34. SECURITY HARDENING — AUTH 2FA + AI PROJECT TENANT ISOLATION (2026-06-13)
================================================================================

A security audit found critical auth-bypass and cross-tenant defects. The
following were fixed and unit-tested (node:test, 52 cases green).

34.1 LOGIN / 2FA BYPASS — FIXED
-------------------------------

  Problems found:
    a) POST /api/auth/complete-login issued a full session from a bare { userId }
       — no proof the password or 2FA step ever happened. Knowing a user UUID = login.
    b) TOTP verification was a no-op: otplib v13 `verify()` returns a Promise, and
       the controller used it as a boolean (always truthy) → EVERY code accepted.
    c) Hardcoded 123456 master code accepted in production on user TOTP, user
       email-OTP, AND admin OTP.

  Fixes:
    - NEW src/services/totp.util.js — verifyTotpToken() uses otplib `verifySync`
      and checks `.valid` (real boolean). isTestOtpAllowed() gates 123456 to
      NODE_ENV !== 'production' only.
    - NEW src/services/auth-2fa-ticket.service.js — two-stage signed JWT ticket
      (scope '2fa'): login issues a PENDING ticket; verify-login / verify-email-otp
      require it + a valid code, then issue a single-use VERIFIED ticket;
      complete-login requires the VERIFIED ticket (jti consumed once).
        * Ticket userId comes from the signed token, never the request body.
        * Stage mismatch / tampered / expired / replayed ticket → 401.
        * complete-login also re-checks isActive + lockedUntil.
    - send-email-otp / verify-login / verify-email-otp now require the PENDING
      ticket (also stops anonymous OTP-email bombing of arbitrary users).
    - admin verify-otp: 123456 gated to non-production (admin in prod = real OTP only).

  New login wire (FE → BE):
    login(email,password,captcha) → { requires2FA, userId, email, twoFactorToken(PENDING) }
    verify-login/email-otp({ code, twoFactorToken:PENDING }) → { twoFactorToken:VERIFIED }
    complete-login({ twoFactorToken:VERIFIED, rememberMe }) → session

  Files: backend src/controllers/{auth,totp,admin}.controller.js,
    src/services/{totp.util,auth-2fa-ticket}.service.js;
    frontend src/services/{auth,totp}.service.js,
    src/features/auth/{login.jsx,OTPVerification.jsx}.
  Tests: tests/unit/auth-2fa.test.js (16), tests/unit/complete-login-guard.test.js (5).

  Still recommended (not in this pass): encrypt twoFactorSecret at rest;
  CSPRNG + Redis-backed OTP store; apply the strict auth rate limiter to
  /api/totp and /api/admin; hard-fail on missing JWT_SECRET in production.

34.2 AI PROJECT CROSS-TENANT ACCESS — FIXED
-------------------------------------------

  Problem: AI-project sub-resource handlers (sources / chats / notes) operated on
  a bare projectId with NO org check, while project-CRUD was org-scoped. A user in
  Org A could read/mutate Org B's project by guessing its UUID — and
  addDocumentSource looked up the document by UUID only, so Org A could attach (and
  DECRYPT) Org B's document into its own project.

  Fixes (backend):
    - ai-project.controller.js: NEW requireProject(req,res,auth) guard runs at the
      top of all 14 sub-resource handlers (listSources, add/remove/retry source,
      list/post/clear chat, list/create/update/delete note). svcGet is org-scoped;
      cross-org id → 404 before any sub-resource work.
    - ai-project.service.js: addDocumentSource() + retryDocumentSource() now take
      orgId and filter the document lookup by organizationId — no cross-tenant decrypt.
    - lint-tenant-queries.js: the 2 AI-project violations it flagged are now clear.

  Files: backend src/controllers/ai-project.controller.js,
    src/services/ai-project.service.js.
  Tests: tests/unit/ai-project-tenant.test.js (9) — every handler 404s cross-org;
    guard scopes by the authenticated org, not the URL/body.

  Out of scope (still flagged by lint-tenant, separate subsystem): document-collab
  + document-comment UUID-only document/folder lookups, and document.controller.js
  OnlyOffice callback. AI-analysis history (getAnalysisHistory) cross-tenant read
  also still open — track separately.

================================================================================
35. SECURITY HARDENING — SSRF + CRYPTO-SHRED + ENCRYPTION + BLOCKCHAIN/QR (2026-06-13)
================================================================================

Second hardening pass from the audit. All items below are implemented and
unit-tested (node:test, 84 cases green: `npm test`).

35.1 SSRF GUARD REWRITE — resolve-and-classify, not string-match
----------------------------------------------------------------

  Problem: url-ingest.service.js (AI Project URL sources) regex-matched the
  hostname string, bypassable via decimal/octal/hex IPs (2130706433, 0177.0.0.1),
  IPv4-mapped IPv6 ([::ffff:127.0.0.1]), DNS names resolving to internal IPs, and
  HTTP redirects to an internal target (only the first URL was checked).

  Fix: NEW src/services/ssrf-guard.js
    - classifyIp(ip): classifies a canonical IP against private/reserved CIDRs:
      IPv4 0/8, 10/8, 100.64/10 (CGNAT), 127/8, 169.254/16 (incl. cloud metadata),
      172.16/12, 192.0.0/24, 192.168/16, 198.18/15, 224/4, 240/4; IPv6 ::1, ::,
      fc00::/7, fe80::/10, and IPv4-mapped (::ffff:*) → embedded v4 classified.
    - assertUrlPublic(url): http/https only; blocks localhost/.local/.internal by
      name; DNS-resolves the host (getaddrinfo canonicalizes the encodings) and
      rejects if ANY resolved address is private.
    - safeFetch(url): redirect='manual' + re-validates EVERY hop (Location) before
      following; caps redirects.
    url-ingest.service.js now calls safeFetch — the bespoke string guard is gone.
  Tests: tests/unit/ssrf-guard.test.js (12).
  Residual (documented): true DNS-rebinding (TTL flip between check and connect)
  is mitigated by per-hop re-resolution but not fully pinned at the socket layer.

35.2 CRYPTO-SHRED — actually destroys keys, fail-closed, recorded
-----------------------------------------------------------------

  Problems: (a) `const isS3Provider = isS3Provider || ...` was a self-referential
  TDZ bug → ciphertext was never deleted on shred; (b) shred tried to delete a
  per-document Vault key `docloq-doc-<id>` that is never created (silent no-op),
  giving false confidence; (c) the crypto_shredding table was never written; (d)
  local-storage ciphertext was never deleted.

  Fix: src/services/archive.service.js cryptoShred()
    - isS3Provider now correctly = (provider === 'minio' || 'r2').
    - The REAL shred primitive is documented + enforced: nulling the per-version
      WRAPPED DEK (documentVersions.encryptionKeyId, also encryptionIv) — the only
      copy needed to derive the plaintext DEK — so the AES-256-GCM ciphertext is
      unrecoverable even if the master/Vault KEK still lives. This step is REQUIRED
      and fail-closed: if it throws, the shred is marked 'failed' and the error is
      surfaced (no false success).
    - Writes a crypto_shredding row (pending → completed/failed) capturing the
      destroyed key IDs + affected counts.
    - Deletes ciphertext for BOTH S3 (bucket-aware) and local disk; deletes the
      Qdrant vector. These are best-effort (erasure already holds once DEK is gone);
      residual failures are recorded in the audit log, not swallowed silently.
    - Removed the bogus per-document Vault key deletion.

35.3 ENCRYPTION HARDENING
-------------------------

  src/services/encryption.service.js:
    - Production refuses to run on an ephemeral random master key: getMasterKey()
      throws if ENCRYPTION_MASTER_KEY is unset and NODE_ENV=production (prevents
      silent unrecoverable data after restart). Dev still allows the ephemeral key.
    - Vault→local fallback is now OPT-IN (ALLOW_LOCAL_KEY_FALLBACK=true). Default:
      when Vault is down in vault mode, the upload FAILS instead of silently
      downgrading to the weaker local key.
    - decryptDocumentKey() dispatches on the key's ACTUAL format (Vault ciphertext
      is "vault:v…"; local is raw base64) via isVaultWrappedKey(), NOT the env var.
      This fixes the silent-downgrade bug: a doc wrapped by the local fallback
      during a Vault outage still decrypts after Vault is restored, and a Vault key
      is never fed to the local unwrapper (or vice-versa). No migration needed.
  Tests: tests/unit/encryption.test.js (6) — round-trip, GCM tamper rejection,
    format dispatch, key/IV uniqueness, production master-key guard.

  DEFERRED (need a versioned ciphertext format + data migration; would break
  existing encrypted docs if applied in place — tracked, not yet done):
    - GCM AAD binding ciphertext↔documentId (blob-swap defense, audit HIGH-3).
    - IV 16→12 bytes (changing ivLength mis-slices existing local-wrapped keys).

35.4 BLOCKCHAIN — Merkle domain separation + real proof verification
--------------------------------------------------------------------

  Problems: the batch Merkle code hashed leaves and nodes identically with sorted
  pairs + last-node duplication (second-preimage / CVE-2012-2459 malleability), and
  the per-document proofs it stored were NEVER verified anywhere — batch-anchored
  docs always fell through to 'db_only'.

  Fix: NEW src/services/merkle.util.js (pure, tested)
    - hashLeaf = keccak256(0x00 || leaf), hashNode = keccak256(0x01 || sort(a,b))
      → distinct domains, no node can pose as a leaf.
    - Lone odd node carried up unchanged (no duplication malleability).
    - verifyMerkleProof(leaf, proof, root) — proofs are now checkable.
    blockchain.service.js uses these and adds verifyDocumentInBatch(contentHash,
    proof, root): verifies the proof locally AND confirms the root exists on-chain
    (contract.verifyBatch). verification.service.js resolveBlockchainVerdict() now
    routes batch-anchored docs through this path → they can reach 'chain_verified'.
  Tests: tests/unit/merkle.test.js (15) — domain separation, proof round-trip for
    n=1..17, non-member/tampered-proof/internal-node-as-leaf rejection.
  NOTE: the deployed contract is unchanged (it only stores roots; the JS scheme is
  self-consistent). updateAnchor() mutability (audit C-2) is by-design for
  auto-anchor-on-edit and left as-is — documented, not changed.

35.5 QR — secret validation, unsigned-QR rejection, QR↔chain inline
-------------------------------------------------------------------

  - qrcode.service.js validates QR_SIGNING_SECRET at boot: a missing/default/short
    secret throws in production (anyone could otherwise forge a valid QR signature),
    warns in dev.
  - verifyByQrImage (document.controller.js): an uploaded QR image with NO DocLoq
    signature is now rejected (status 'UNSIGNED', verified=false). A genuine QR
    always embeds {payload, sig}; a bare shortCode is not proof of authenticity.
    (Invalid signature → 'TAMPERED' as before.)
  - QR↔blockchain efficiency: verifyByShortCode + verifyByQrImage responses now
    include a top-level `blockchain` summary { state, verified, network, txHash,
    explorerUrl, onChainHash, anchoredAt } derived from the on-chain read
    (attachChainVerdict → resolveBlockchainVerdict, which reads the CHAIN, not the
    DB). One QR scan returns authenticity + version + blockchain verdict in a single
    call — no extra /api/blockchain round-trip.

35.6 NEW ENV VARS
-----------------

  ALLOW_LOCAL_KEY_FALLBACK   (default false) — allow local-key fallback when Vault
                             is unreachable in vault mode. Off = fail-closed.
  QR_SIGNING_SECRET          now REQUIRED (≥32 chars, non-default) in production.
  ENCRYPTION_MASTER_KEY      now REQUIRED in production (no ephemeral fallback).

35.7 FILES
----------

  New:      backend/src/services/ssrf-guard.js, merkle.util.js
            backend/tests/unit/{ssrf-guard,merkle,encryption}.test.js
  Modified: backend/src/services/{url-ingest,archive,encryption,blockchain,
            verification,qrcode}.service.js
            backend/src/controllers/document.controller.js
            backend/src/config/upload.config.js (env doc only)

================================================================================
36. PUBLIC PREVIEW-ONLY SHARE LINKS (2026-06-14)
================================================================================

Share a document via a public link (like Google Docs link-sharing) — but
preview-only and security-first. Design spec: infra/specs/2026-06-14-public-
preview-share-links-design.md.

36.1 WHAT IT DOES
-----------------

  An authorized org member generates a public link. Anyone with the link can
  PREVIEW the document in the browser — no login, no download, no edit. The
  document stays AES-256-GCM encrypted at rest; the server decrypts in memory only
  to render page images. Viewers receive flattened, visibly-watermarked PNGs — the
  raw file bytes NEVER reach the browser.

  MVP scope (fixed, not configurable): public + preview-only. Edit-via-link and
  download-via-link are explicitly out of scope.

  IMPORTANT — what actually gets rendered: DocLoq's upload pipeline encrypts the
  honeytoken-modified EXTRACTED TEXT for text-extractable files (upload-pipeline.
  service.js: "Encrypting honeytoken-modified content"), so most documents decrypt
  to text, not their original binary. The preview renderer is therefore CONTENT-
  AWARE: it detects the decrypted buffer kind and renders accordingly —
    text  → clean paginated A4 page images drawn via @napi-rs/canvas (invisible
            honeytoken/watermark chars stripped + homoglyph substitutions reversed
            so the text shows clean, no markers leak),
    PDF   → pages rendered via pdfjs (the rarer hasText=false case),
    image → the image itself.
  So preview works for every document (previewable is effectively always true).

36.2 DATA MODEL — NO MIGRATION
------------------------------

  Reuses the existing documentShares + documentShareAccess tables (§4 #22, #23):
    documentShares: shareType='view_only', requireAuth=false, shareToken =
      crypto.randomBytes(32) base64url (unguessable), shareUrl, expiresAt
      (default now+7d), maxViews (optional), viewCount, isActive, createdBy.
    documentShareAccess: shareId, ipAddress, userAgent, accessedAt (one row per
      preview session — the only viewer identity for an anonymous link).

36.3 SECURITY MODEL
-------------------

  - The public endpoint serves ONLY the one document the share points to, and only
    while active + not expired + under maxViews. The share is an explicit grant by
    an org member who already has access — org isolation is preserved, not bypassed.
  - Doc stays encrypted at rest; decrypt is in-memory, render-only; no raw bytes to
    the browser (only PNG pages). Rendered pages held in a bounded in-memory LRU
    cache (no decrypted artifacts written to disk).
  - Visible diagonal watermark baked into every page: "DocLoq Preview · <share8> ·
    <YYYY-MM-DD>" — a leaked screenshot traces back to the specific share → creator
    + access log.
  - Audit: every first-page fetch writes a documentShareAccess row + bumps viewCount.
  - Revoke is instant (isActive=false → 410). Default 7-day expiry caps exposure.
  - "No download" is enforced by never shipping the file (only PNGs); screenshots
    remain possible — mitigated by the watermark + access log, not prevented.

36.4 BACKEND
------------

  New services:
    src/services/share.service.js          createShare / listShares / revokeShare /
      resolveShare (gates active/expiry/maxViews) / recordAccess. Typed errors
      ShareNotFoundError (→404), ShareGoneError (→410). Org-scoped create/list/revoke.
    src/services/share-preview.service.js   getManifest (no sensitive fields) +
      renderPage (decrypt in memory → detectKind(buffer) → render: text via canvas /
      PDF via ocr.service extractPageAsImage / image via sharp → composite diagonal
      watermark + downscale to 1240px → bounded in-memory LRU cache). Helpers:
      detectKind(buffer), stripInvisible(text) (strips markers + reverses homoglyphs).

  New controller/routes:
    src/controllers/share.controller.js
    src/routes/share.routes.js  (mounted at /api/shares)

  API:
    POST   /api/documents/:id/shares     [auth, org]  create  → { shareToken, shareUrl, expiresAt, maxViews }
    GET    /api/documents/:id/shares     [auth, org]  list    → [{ token, expiresAt, viewCount, status, ... }]
    DELETE /api/shares/:shareId          [auth, org]  revoke
    GET    /api/shares/:token            [public]     manifest → { docName, mimeType, pageCount, previewable, expiresAt }
    GET    /api/shares/:token/page/:n    [public]     watermarked PNG (records access on page 1)
  Error contract: 410 gone/expired/over-limit · 404 purged/unknown · 415 not previewable.

36.5 FRONTEND
-------------

  New:
    src/services/share.service.js                 API client (+ pageUrl for <img>)
    src/features/documents/ShareModal.jsx          create + manage links; amber
      preview-only callout; expiry pills (7d/30d/no-limit) + optional max-views;
      copy button; active-link list with revoke (ConfirmModal). lucide icons, no emoji.
    src/features/share/SharePreview.jsx            public /share/:token viewer —
      standalone branded page, lazy-loaded watermarked page cards, scroll progress,
      Preview badge, states (loading/gone/notfound/not-previewable), no download/print.
  Modified:
    src/app/routes/index.jsx                       + public route /share/:token
    src/features/documents/Documents.jsx           + "Bagikan" button in the document
      detail modal → opens ShareModal.

  Theme: editorial-restrained (bg-white/dark:slate-900, stone/slate borders,
  rounded-2xl, indigo-600 accent, framer-motion [0.22,1,0.36,1]); lucide-react icons
  only; full dark mode; Indonesian copy. No emoji.

36.6 TESTS
----------

  tests/unit/share.test.js (9) — token entropy/format/uniqueness, computeExpiry
  (null/zero/negative → no expiry; positive → ~N days), shareStatus gating
  (active/revoked/expired/exhausted, precedence), detectKind (pdf/image magic →
  text fallback), stripInvisible (markers removed + homoglyphs reversed). Full
  suite: 93 cases green (`npm test`).

  Verified end-to-end on the local stack (Postgres + Redis via docker compose,
  KEY_PROVIDER=local, STORAGE_PROVIDER=local): register → upload PDF → create share
  → public manifest (200) → render page (200, image/png, valid watermarked PNG) →
  access logged (viewCount=1) → bad token (404) → revoke (200) → manifest (410).
  Frontend production build passes (vite, 3087 modules).

36.7 FOLLOW-UPS (documented, not in MVP)
----------------------------------------

  - Faithful original-layout preview (current text-doc preview renders the stored
    TEXT content, not the original visual layout — would need storing the original
    binary alongside the honeytoken'd text, or OnlyOffice office→PDF rendering).
  - Version pinning (preview a fixed version, not current) — needs a versionId column.
  - Email/login-gated and download/edit-capable shares (Phase 2).
  - Per-route rate limit dedicated to /api/shares/* (currently the global limiter).

37. SETTINGS AUDIT FIXES — SESSIONS / PROFILE / ROLES / 2FA (2026-06-14)
================================================================================

A full audit of the Settings area (tenant + per-user binding) found the reported
"another user's login shows MY MacBook session" bug plus several gaps. All four
were fixed and verified.

37.1 ACTIVE SESSIONS — the reported bug (was a hardcoded mock)
--------------------------------------------------------------

  Cause: the "Active Sessions" panel in Settings.jsx was HARDCODED JSX
  ("MacBook Pro - Chrome / Current session") with no backend source — so every
  user on every device saw it. Not a data leak; fabricated UI.

  Fix: real, per-user endpoints (auth.controller.js + auth.routes.js):
    GET    /api/auth/sessions        list caller's own sessions (userId from JWT;
                                     each row: userAgent, ipAddress, lastActivityAt,
                                     createdAt, expiresAt, current flag). Token never
                                     exposed; `current` = row.token === request token.
    DELETE /api/auth/sessions/:id    revoke one own session (ownership enforced:
                                     where id AND userId → 404 if not yours).
    DELETE /api/auth/sessions        sign out all OTHER devices (keep current).
  Frontend Settings.jsx: panel now fetches real sessions, parses a friendly device
  label from the user-agent, shows IP + last-active, "Sesi ini" badge for the
  current device, per-session "Keluar", and "Keluar dari perangkat lain".

37.2 PROFILE SAVE — was a stub
------------------------------

  Cause: handleProfileUpdate only console.log'd; profileForm used hardcoded
  "John Doe" fallbacks and never re-synced to the loaded user.
  Fix: new PATCH /api/auth/me (updateMe) — self-update of firstName/lastName/phone/
  position/departmentId only (role/email/org/isActive NOT editable). Settings.jsx
  now calls it, updates the auth store, and a useEffect re-syncs profileForm from
  the live user (no stale/placeholder identity on a new login).

37.3 ROLES — cross-tenant gaps (HIGH ×2)
----------------------------------------

  role.controller.js:
    - createRole / updateRole / assignUsersToRole now run assigned userIds through
      filterOrgUserIds(userIds, organizationId) — only users in the caller's org are
      assigned (was: arbitrary userId accepted → foreign-tenant user injected into a
      role's permissions).
    - getUserPermissions now scopes the target-user lookup by organizationId
      (was: a tenant-A admin could probe a tenant-B user's role / hasFullAccess).

37.4 2FA DISABLE — password now required
----------------------------------------

  totp.controller.js disableTOTP now requires the account password (bcrypt.compare)
  in addition to a valid TOTP code — a hijacked session can no longer drop 2FA with
  just a live code. Settings.jsx disable modal adds a password field.

37.5 DEPARTMENTS — defense-in-depth
-----------------------------------

  department.controller.js update/delete where-clauses now include organizationId
  (previously relied on a prior 404 existence check only).

37.6 CONFIRMED ALREADY TENANT/USER-SAFE (no change needed)
----------------------------------------------------------

  user.controller (all queries org-scoped; new users inherit creator org; sessions
  invalidated on deactivate/delete/reset), company/org profile (org-from-token, no
  IDOR), notifications (per-userId), TOTP status/generate/enable (per-user), auth
  login/completeLogin/logout/getMe + issueSession (session bound to
  userId+userAgent+ipAddress; logout deletes only the current token). The Zustand
  auth store fully overwrites the previous user on login and clears on logout.

37.7 VERIFICATION
-----------------

  Backend unit suite: 93/93. Frontend build: clean. Live DB controller test (11/11):
  per-user session isolation (u2 sessions share no id with u1), revoke ownership
  (cross-user → 404, self → 200 + row gone), updateMe (changes name, ignores role
  escalation), disableTOTP gate (no-password 400 / wrong 401 / correct 200),
  getUserPermissions cross-org → 404 / same-org → 200. Files: auth.controller.js,
  auth.routes.js, role.controller.js, totp.controller.js, department.controller.js;
  frontend Settings.jsx, services/{auth,totp}.service.js.

38. TEMPLATE/IMAGE OPEN FIX — PIPELINE BINARY PRESERVATION + OCR CRASH (2026-06-16)
================================================================================

Reported: "upload-from-template doesn't work at all and won't open in OnlyOffice;
image upload can't be opened." Audit traced ALL of these to ONE upload-pipeline
defect (plus an OCR crash bug found while testing).

38.1 ROOT CAUSE — pipeline stored extracted TEXT instead of the file
--------------------------------------------------------------------

  upload-pipeline.service.js STEP 8/10 replaced the file with honeytoken-modified
  EXTRACTED TEXT whenever any text was extractable (`hasText && normalizedText`) —
  true for DOCX (mammoth), PDF (pdfjs/OCR), AND images (OCR). So the encrypted/
  served bytes were plain text, not the original binary:
    - DOCX template  → served text → OnlyOffice cannot open (expects a .docx zip)
    - image          → served OCR text → image viewer gets text, not the image
    - upload-from-template appeared "completely broken" (result not openable)

  Fix: encrypt the ORIGINAL file buffer by default; substitute the honeytoken text
  ONLY for genuine plain-text files (text/plain, text/csv, text/markdown), where
  "text in == file out". Binary formats keep their bytes intact. The honeytoken
  RECORD is still stored for forensics; binary leak-tracing already happens at
  download time via document-rewriter (DOCX XML / PDF annotation watermark).

38.2 OCR CRASH — a corrupt image took down the whole backend
------------------------------------------------------------

  An undecodable/corrupt image made the tesseract.js worker emit an async error
  re-thrown on process.nextTick (uncatchable by try/catch) → uncaught exception →
  the entire backend process exited. Fix: ocr.service.js processImageForAI now
  HARD-validates + normalizes the image through sharp (`sharp(buf).rotate().png()`)
  BEFORE any Tesseract call; an undecodable image throws there and is caught by
  extractText's image branch (→ hasText=false → original binary stored). Tesseract
  only ever receives a sharp-decoded PNG, so it can no longer crash the server.

38.3 TEMPLATE FLOW (was already wired, now actually works)
----------------------------------------------------------

  create-blank and upload-file both run the document through the full upload
  pipeline (createDocumentRecord → uploadPipeline) and place it in the org's
  "Template Forms" root folder (getOrCreateTemplateFolder, idempotent). A forms
  row links to the document via schema.documentId. getTemplateDocument(:id/document)
  returns the OnlyOffice config pointing at /api/documents/:id/file. PDF templates
  are converted to DOCX via OnlyOffice first (needs the OnlyOffice service up).
  Image documents open in the in-app viewer (Documents.jsx openDocumentSmart →
  viewerDoc → <img>), NOT OnlyOffice — that routing was already correct; it only
  failed because the served bytes were text (fixed by 38.1).

38.4 VERIFICATION
-----------------

  Backend unit suite: 93/93. Live E2E (11/11, real running stack): create-blank
  template → served /file is a valid DOCX; getTemplateDocument → 200 + config;
  upload-file template (real DOCX) → 201 + served valid DOCX; template lands in
  "Template Forms" folder; valid PNG upload → served valid PNG; CORRUPT image →
  422 graceful AND backend stays UP (no crash).

  Files: backend src/services/upload-pipeline.service.js (binary preservation),
  src/services/ocr.service.js (sharp pre-validation). Existing documents stored as
  text before this fix must be re-uploaded to become openable again.

38.5 AI-PROJECT GRANT — "some documents can't be granted" (2026-06-16)
---------------------------------------------------------------------

  grantAIAccess (ai-analysis.service.js) decrypts → extractText → indexes to Qdrant.
  It only OCR'd image/* mimes, so several document kinds failed to grant:
    - scanned / image-only PDFs (no text layer, not image mime) → "Cannot extract text"
    - legacy docs stored as TEXT before §38.1 (extractText parsed text-as-binary → empty)
    - images stored as text by the old pipeline
  Fix: new extractTextForGrant() unifies extraction with fallbacks —
    extractText → (PDF with no text) detectContentType + page-render OCR
    (extractTextFromPages) → (image) processImageForAI → (printable buffer)
    use the raw decrypted text (recovers legacy text-stored docs). Only if ALL fail
    does it reject, with a clear Indonesian message.
  Verified live (3/3): grant DOCX (text) → 200; grant image-with-text (OCR) → 200.
  File: backend src/services/ai-analysis.service.js.


================================================================================
39. TRASH PERMANENT-DELETE + FORM TEMPLATE FIXES (2026-06-19)
================================================================================

39.1 TRASH — "permanent delete failed terus" (only restore worked)
------------------------------------------------------------------

  Symptom: a document in Trash could be restored but never permanently deleted —
  "Delete forever" failed every time, while Restore worked fine.

  Root cause: permanentDelete ran a bare `DELETE FROM documents WHERE id = ?`.
  Eight tables reference documents.id WITHOUT an onDelete policy, so Postgres
  rejects the delete with a foreign-key violation whenever ANY child row exists:
    tasks.relatedDocumentId, form_instances.generatedDocumentId,
    form_submissions.generatedDocumentId, temporary_uploads.resultDocumentId,
    security_events.relatedDocumentId, leak_scans.documentId,
    leak_reports.documentId, honeytoken_triggers.documentId (NOT NULL).
  The 500 surfaced in the UI as a generic "Action failed". Restore only flips
  deletedAt → null, deletes nothing, so it never hit the constraint.

  Fix: new hardDeleteDocument(tx, docId) helper runs inside a db.transaction —
  it NULLs the references that outlive the document (tasks, form_instances,
  form_submissions, temporary_uploads, security_events, leak_scans, leak_reports)
  and DELETEs honeytoken_triggers (NOT NULL → cannot detach) before deleting the
  document. Cascade-configured children (document_versions, signatures, …) are
  removed by the DB. Applied to BOTH permanentDelete and emptyTrash; the trash_item
  row is removed in the same transaction so the operation is atomic.
  File: backend src/controllers/trash.controller.js.

39.2 FORM TEMPLATE — uploads but won't open/save in OnlyOffice (PRIMARY)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------

  Symptom: a form template uploaded successfully but could not be opened or saved
  in the OnlyOffice editor.

  Root cause: getTemplateDocument built the editor config WITHOUT the signed
  oo_token on the /file and /callback URLs. The /api/documents/:id/file and
  /callback endpoints use optionalAuth and require EITHER a user Bearer token OR a
  valid oo_token. OnlyOffice (the server, not the user's browser) fetches/posts
  with neither → 401 on /file (cannot load) and {error:1} on /callback (cannot
  save). Normal documents include the token; the template editor was copy-pasted
  but the token was left out.

  Fix: generateOOToken is now exported from document.controller.js and imported by
  form.controller.js (MUST reuse the same function — OO_SECRET falls back to a
  per-process random secret when ONLYOFFICE_SECRET is unset, so a re-implemented
  copy would never verify). getTemplateDocument appends
  ?oo_token=<token> to both the /file and /callback URLs, matching the document
  editor. Files: backend src/controllers/document.controller.js (export),
  src/controllers/form.controller.js (getTemplateDocument).

39.3 FORM TEMPLATE — cross-organization access (authorization bypass)
---------------------------------------------------------------------

  updateFormTemplate and deleteFormTemplate looked up the form with
  `where(eq(forms.id, id))` only — no organization scoping. An authenticated user
  from org A could edit or trash any template from org B by guessing its UUID.
  Fix: both now resolve the caller's orgId and scope every SELECT/UPDATE with
  `and(eq(forms.id, id), eq(forms.organizationId, orgId))`, matching the read
  paths. File: backend src/controllers/form.controller.js.

39.4 FORM TEMPLATE — upload rejected / generic 500
--------------------------------------------------

  The multer fileFilter matched on mimetype only and threw a bare Error on a miss,
  which bubbled to the global handler as a generic 500. Browsers/OSes sometimes
  send office files as application/octet-stream, so valid .docx uploads were
  rejected.
  Fix: fileFilter now also accepts application/octet-stream and text/rtf and falls
  back to the file extension (.pdf/.docx/.doc/.odt/.rtf). A uploadTemplateFile
  wrapper converts multer/fileFilter errors into a clear 400 JSON response
  (e.g. "File too large (max 50MB)") instead of a 500.
  File: backend src/routes/form.routes.js.

  Note (not a bug): the earlier-suspected "missing await resolveOrgId" is harmless —
  resolveOrgId is synchronous (returns req.user?.organizationId || null); awaiting a
  non-promise changes nothing. The real access failure was the missing oo_token (§39.2).
  Cosmetic awaits were normalized for consistency only.

  Operational reminder: Vault re-seals on every restart. If document upload/edit
  still fails after these fixes, check the Vault seal status FIRST — a sealed Vault
  breaks decryption independently of the bugs above.

  Verification: node --check on all edited files; full ESM import graph resolves
  with no circular import; generateOOToken is exported and produces a valid
  3-part token.


================================================================================
40. SHARE PREVIEW — OFFICE DOCS RENDERED BLANK FIX (2026-06-19)
================================================================================

  Symptom: opening a public share link (/share/:token) showed a blank page — the
  viewer loaded but no file content was visible.

  Root cause: a regression against §38 (binary preservation). share-preview.service
  was written assuming the decrypted document is TEXT, PDF, or an image, and
  detectKind() falls through to 'text' for anything else. After §38 the pipeline
  stores office documents (DOCX/XLSX/PPTX, legacy .doc/.xls/.ppt, ODF, RTF) as
  their original BINARY. detectKind only recognized %PDF and image magic bytes, so
  an OOXML/ZIP (50 4B), OLE2 (D0 CF 11 E0) or RTF ({\rt) document was classified
  'text' and renderTextPage() drew its raw binary as UTF-8 → a blank/garbled page.
  PDFs, images and genuine plain-text (.txt/.csv/.md) shares were unaffected.

  Fix (backend src/services/share-preview.service.js):
    - detectKind() now returns a new 'office' kind for ZIP/OOXML/ODF (50 4B …),
      OLE2 legacy office (D0 CF 11 E0 A1 B1 1A E1) and RTF ({\rt).
    - buildContext() converts 'office' documents to PDF via OnlyOffice
      (convertDocument) BEFORE rendering, then treats the result exactly like a
      PDF (pdfjs → page images). The OnlyOffice source URL reuses the document's
      existing decrypted /api/documents/:id/file endpoint with a signed oo_token —
      /uploads static serving was removed in SEC-B-011, so no temp file is staged.
      generateOOToken is imported from document.controller.js (must be the SAME
      function — OO_SECRET falls back to a per-process random secret).
    - If conversion fails (OnlyOffice down, unsupported format) the kind becomes
      'unsupported'; getManifest returns previewable:false so the viewer shows a
      clean "Belum bisa di-preview" message instead of a blank/garbled page, and
      renderPage throws NOT_PREVIEWABLE (HTTP 415). The converted PDF is cached in
      ctxCache (one conversion per document).

  Not a bug: shareUrl is built as `${FRONTEND_URL}/share/${token}` in
  share.service.js — correct as long as FRONTEND_URL is set in the backend env. If
  a recipient's link points at localhost, check FRONTEND_URL on the server.

  Verification: node --check passes; ESM import graph resolves with no circular
  import (document.controller does not import share-preview); detectKind unit
  checks pass for PDF/PNG/DOCX-zip/OLE2/RTF/plain-text.

================================================================================
41. VIEWER/EDITOR UX + SHARE PREVIEW RENDER FIXES (2026-06-20)
================================================================================

A batch of reported document bugs (PDF preview, form styling, blockchain toggle,
form-template editing, download naming) plus PDF inline editing and two share-
preview production failures. All code-verified (frontend vite build clean; backend
node --check). The share PDF render crash was reproduced 1:1 locally with the
backend's own deps before fixing.

41.1 PDF PREVIEW SHOWED RAW BYTES ("hex") — blob MIME + wrong endpoint
----------------------------------------------------------------------

  Symptom: opening a PDF in the in-app viewer showed garbled hex/binary, not the PDF.

  Root cause: documentService.getFileBlobUrl wrapped the axios response in
  `new Blob([response.data])` WITHOUT a type → a typeless blob URL; an <iframe> then
  rendered the PDF bytes as text. It also fetched /documents/:id/download
  (Content-Disposition: attachment + a download watermark + an audit-log write on
  EVERY preview) — the wrong endpoint for inline viewing.

  Fix (frontend): getFileBlobUrl(id, mimeType) now fetches /documents/:id/file
  (inline serve, no watermark/audit side-effect) and tags the Blob with the real
  mimeType so the browser renders the PDF/image. Documents.jsx passes
  viewerDoc.mimeType. Files: frontend src/services/document.service.js,
  src/features/documents/Documents.jsx.

41.2 NEW-TEMPLATE FORM FIELDS INVISIBLE ON FOCUS (dark mode)
-----------------------------------------------------------

  Symptom: in the "Upload Template" / new-form modal, focusing the Name/Description/
  Notes fields turned them white with white text → unreadable.

  Root cause: every field had `focus:bg-white` with no dark-mode counterpart, so on
  focus the dark theme got a white background under white text.
  Fix: added `dark:focus:bg-slate-800` to all 9 occurrences.
  File: frontend src/features/forms/Forms.jsx.

41.3 BLOCKCHAIN AUTO-ANCHOR TOGGLE OFF-DESIGN
---------------------------------------------

  The "Auto-anchor on edit" switch used a generic gray off-state and no a11y. Rebuilt
  as a proper role="switch" (aria-checked/label), violet on-state, focus-visible
  ring, cleaner geometry. File: frontend src/features/documents/Documents.jsx.

41.4 FORM-TEMPLATE EDITOR OPENED READ-ONLY (OnlyOffice key collision)
--------------------------------------------------------------------

  Symptom: editing worked for normal Documents but NOT for form templates — the
  editor opened but you could not type (even .docx).

  Root cause: the Forms flow opens the template in VIEW first (card click), then
  EDIT (button). getTemplateDocument reused the SAME OnlyOffice document `key`
  (`${doc.id}-${updatedAt}`) for both modes, so OnlyOffice served the cached
  read-only VIEW session on the switch to edit. It also set autosave/forcesave:false
  (edits wouldn't persist) and left dashes in key/user.id, diverging from the working
  document.controller.js config.

  Fix (backend): getTemplateDocument now mirrors the document editor config —
  dashless, version-based key SUFFIXED with the mode (`${id}_v${ver}_${mode}`) so
  VIEW and EDIT never share a cached session; autosave/forcesave gated on
  mode==='edit'; callbackUrl always set; user.id dashless.
  Fix (frontend): <OnlyOfficeEditor> in both Documents.jsx and Forms.jsx now has a
  key tied to `${docId}_${mode}`, so a view→edit switch remounts the editor in edit
  mode instead of reusing the stale view instance.
  Files: backend src/controllers/form.controller.js; frontend
  src/features/documents/Documents.jsx, src/features/forms/Forms.jsx.

41.5 DOWNLOAD FILENAME "unknown"
-------------------------------

  Symptom: downloaded documents saved as "unknown".

  Root cause: the frontend ignored the server's Content-Disposition filename and used
  a caller-passed name that was sometimes undefined; the API is on a different
  subdomain so the browser also could not read Content-Disposition (not exposed via
  CORS).
  Fix: backend CORS now sets exposedHeaders: ['Content-Disposition'];
  downloadDocument/downloadDocumentAs parse the filename from the header (RFC5987 +
  plain) and fall back to the passed name / `document-<id>` — never undefined.
  Files: backend src/index.js; frontend src/services/document.service.js.

41.6 PDF EDITING — transparent convert-to-DOCX on Edit
------------------------------------------------------

  PDFs were view-only. New: hitting Edit on a PDF transparently converts it to an
  editable DOCX (no separate convert button).

  Backend: NEW POST /api/documents/:id/convert-to-editable (authenticate) — converts
  the PDF to DOCX via the OnlyOffice ConvertService (source fetched through the signed
  /file URL), stores the DOCX as a NEW encrypted version (same pipeline as the
  OnlyOffice save path: per-version DEK, meta.json authTag, DNA recompute), and flips
  the document to DOCX (mimeType + originalFilename). The original PDF stays in
  version history. QR is superseded+regenerated and the doc re-anchored if
  autoAnchorOnEdit — identical to an edit-save, so verification stays consistent.
  No-op (converted:false) for already-editable formats; 409 if another user holds the
  edit lock.
  Frontend: documentService.convertToEditable; the PDF detail-modal footer shows
  "Pratinjau" + "Edit"; Edit shows a "Mengonversi PDF ke DOCX…" overlay, then opens
  the (now DOCX) document in the editor.
  Files: backend src/controllers/document.controller.js,
  src/routes/document.routes.js; frontend src/services/document.service.js,
  src/features/documents/Documents.jsx.

41.7 UNIFIED PREVIEW-THEN-EDIT (double-click) + Edit in the viewer
------------------------------------------------------------------

  Double-clicking a document now opens PREVIEW for ALL types (was: office went
  straight to edit): PDF/image → in-app viewer; office (docx/xlsx/pptx/txt) →
  OnlyOffice VIEW mode (which carries its own switch-to-edit button). The in-app
  viewer header gained an "Edit" action next to "Unduh" (PDF → convert-to-edit;
  hidden for images, which have no edit). handlePreviewDocument() routes by type.
  File: frontend src/features/documents/Documents.jsx.

41.8 PUBLIC SHARE PREVIEW — blank, then 500 (two distinct causes)
-----------------------------------------------------------------

  Symptom: /share/:token loaded the chrome ("Halaman 1") but no page image; later a
  500 on /api/shares/:token/page/:n.

  Cause A — CORP block: the page PNG is loaded as an <img> from api.docloq.site into
  the docloq.site frontend (different subdomain). Helmet's default
  Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin blocked the cross-origin <img> (the
  manifest worked because it is a CORS fetch). The page never rendered.
  Fix: helmet({ crossOriginResourcePolicy: { policy: 'cross-origin' } }) — API
  resources stay gated by auth/share tokens; only the CORP embedding rule is relaxed.
  File: backend src/index.js.

  Cause B — pdfjs render crash on Alpine: with CORP fixed, page/:n returned 500
  "Failed to render PDF page 1 as image: Value is none of these types `String`,
  `Path`, `Buffer`". ocr.service.extractPageAsImage rendered with useSystemFonts:true
  + disableFontFace:true. A ReportLab PDF's base-14 fonts (Helvetica) are NOT
  embedded; on the Alpine container there is no system Helvetica, so pdfjs fell back
  to char-by-char glyph painting of standard-font data, which throws a NATIVE
  @napi-rs/canvas error in paintChar. It only worked on a dev box because a system
  font was found.
  A first attempt changed the font config (bundled standard_fonts + disableFontFace
  variants). It rendered fine on a Windows dev box but STILL crashed on the Alpine
  container — confirmed from live production logs: BOTH the standard-fonts primary
  and the path-glyph fallback throw the same native error. Root cause is the musl
  @napi-rs/canvas binary crashing while painting pdfjs glyph paths — independent of
  the font config. (Image-based/scanned PDFs render fine because they draw an
  embedded image, not glyph paths — which is why OCR rendering "worked on Alpine".)

  Real fix (backend src/services/share-preview.service.js): renderPage() wraps the
  pdfjs page-image render in try/catch; on failure it falls back to
  renderPdfPageAsText() — pdfjs getTextContent() for that page (text extraction
  works on Alpine) drawn as a clean page image via basic canvas fillText (the same
  text-document path that already renders on Alpine). Page-aligned with the PDF so
  pageCount stays consistent. Text PDFs that crash the glyph renderer now show their
  text content (watermarked); image-based PDFs still render pixel-perfect via pdfjs.
  Verified locally: parsePdf extracts the page text and the basic-canvas render
  produces a valid PNG. (ocr.service.extractPageAsImage keeps the standard_fonts
  config — harmless, and it's the function that throws into the fallback.)

41.9 VERIFICATION + DEPLOYMENT
-----------------------------

  - Frontend: vite build clean. Backend: node --check on all edited files.
  - Share PDF render fix verified by reproducing the exact production error locally
    and confirming the patched extractPageAsImage renders a valid PNG.
  - No DB migration. The PDF→DOCX convert adds a normal new document version.
  - The share fix requires a backend REDEPLOY (helmet + ocr.service changes).
  Files — backend: src/index.js, src/services/ocr.service.js,
    src/controllers/{document,form}.controller.js, src/routes/document.routes.js;
    frontend: src/services/document.service.js,
    src/features/documents/Documents.jsx, src/features/forms/Forms.jsx.

================================================================================
42. ROLE REFACTOR — OWNER / ADMIN / USER (2026-06-20)
================================================================================

Tenant user roles collapsed from six (super_admin, admin, manager, user, auditor,
viewer) to three with distinct capabilities. The platform `adminUsers` /
`adminRoleEnum` system (admin_users table, OTP login) is SEPARATE and untouched.

42.1 ROLE MODEL
---------------

  - owner  — company owner / PIC. Everything admin can do PLUS the trust/security
             features: blockchain anchoring, in-app document verification, tracking
             (OSINT/watermark-scanner). Exactly ONE owner per tenant.
  - admin  — delegated manager: user management + role management only (NOT
             blockchain / verification / OSINT).
  - user   — regular member; document access by per-document/folder permission.

  Capability matrix (owner / admin / user):
    document CRUD            full / full / per-permission
    user + role management    yes /  yes /  no   (admin cannot grant owner)
    blockchain anchoring      yes /   no /  no
    in-app verification       yes /   no /  no   (QR-scan is owner-only too, §66.C)
    tracking / OSINT          yes /   no /  no
    AI analysis/projects/Doki yes /  yes /  yes  (per-tenant on/off — see §44)

42.2 SCHEMA + MIGRATION
-----------------------

  userRoleEnum (db/schema.js): ['super_admin','admin','manager','user','auditor',
  'viewer'] → ['owner','admin','user']. Hand-written migration
  drizzle/0013_roles_owner_admin_user.sql (Postgres enum removal needs a type swap):
    - column → text, remap, recreate enum, column → new enum. Transactional.
    - Remap: earliest-created user per org → owner; legacy super_admin → owner;
      manager/auditor/viewer/user → user; admin stays admin.
  Applied + verified on production: BEFORE admin=11/manager=1/user=2 →
  AFTER owner=10/admin=1/user=3; every org has exactly one owner (bad=0). A
  `users_role_bak` snapshot of pre-migration roles was kept for safety.

42.3 OWNERSHIP TRANSFER
-----------------------

  New endpoint POST /api/users/:id/transfer-ownership (owner-only, atomic): target
  becomes owner, caller demoted to admin (enforces the one-owner invariant).
  createUser/updateUser accept only user|admin; owner is assigned ONLY via transfer.
  Registration: the founder of a new org is created as `owner` (was `admin`).

42.4 AUTHORIZATION CHANGES (exhaustive)
---------------------------------------

  Backend authorize() gates:
    blockchain.routes, osint.routes, watermark-scanner.routes → ['owner']
    user/role/department/organization routes, document & folder permission routes,
      permission.middleware full-access → ['owner','admin']
  Controllers/services remapped (user, role, dashboard, task, document-collab,
  ai-project, document-comment, ai-analysis, chatbot). In-app verification routes
  gated to owner via the UI/auth context. (The QR-scan endpoints were left open at
  the time; they were closed later — see §66.C.)

  Frontend: scattered `role==='admin'||'super_admin'` replaced with capability
  checks — canManage(owner+admin) for user/role/dept mgmt; owner-only for
  blockchain panel, OSINT nav, verification. Sites: auth.store, DashboardLayout
  (OSINT nav `ownerOnly`), Documents (blockchain → owner), Settings (role dropdown
  → admin/user, labels), OnlyOfficeEditor, CommentModal, RequirePermission default,
  dev-bypass.

  Non-regression: a repo grep confirms no remaining tenant-role literal
  (super_admin/manager/auditor/viewer) outside the admin_users system and the
  document permission-levels (none/viewer/editor/admin — a different concept). The
  recently-fixed PDF/preview/convert/share flows are role-independent and unaffected.

================================================================================
43. AUTOMATED MIGRATIONS — CUSTOM RUNNER (2026-06-20)
================================================================================

  Problem: entrypoint.sh ran `npx drizzle-kit migrate`, but this repo keeps NO
  drizzle meta/_journal.json — so drizzle-kit applied nothing and hand-written .sql
  migrations (incl. data migrations) had to be run by hand.

  Fix: src/scripts/migrate.js — a small runner that applies drizzle/*.sql in
  filename order, tracked in a `_migrations` table (filename + applied_at), each in
  its own transaction, skipping already-applied files. Auto-baseline: on an
  already-provisioned DB (users table exists) with empty tracking, all current files
  are recorded as applied WITHOUT re-running them; a fresh DB runs them all.

  Wiring: package.json `db:migrate` → `node src/scripts/migrate.js`; entrypoint.sh
  runs it on the migration leader (RUN_MIGRATIONS=true) before the app starts.
  Production `_migrations` was pre-seeded with the 15 existing files (0000–0013), and
  the runner was confirmed on deploy logging `[migrate] up to date`.

  Going forward: add a numbered `drizzle/00XX_*.sql` (hand-written; data migrations
  welcome) → on deploy it auto-applies. drizzle-kit generate is not used (no journal).

44. SUPER-ADMIN DASHBOARD AT /kicawkicaw (2026-06-20)
================================================================================

  The platform operator's console: platform stats, tenant management, and per-tenant
  feature control. Replaces the old tenant-create form at /kicawkicaw (the create
  form is kept as a tab). Separate from the OTP `/admin/dashboard` system.

44.1 AUTH — SHARED-SECRET GATE
------------------------------

  Backend env ADMIN_GATE_SECRET (default 'IndonesiaRaya' to match the frontend
  VITE_TENANT_GATE default). The /kicawkicaw password the operator types is sent as
  header `X-Admin-Gate` on every /api/superadmin/* call; middleware requireAdminGate
  (constant-time compare) guards all of them. CORS exposes the X-Admin-Gate header.
  SECURITY: a static secret is weak for terminate / feature / data actions —
  set ADMIN_GATE_SECRET + VITE_TENANT_GATE to the same CUSTOM value in production;
  migrating to the OTP admin auth is recommended.

44.2 PER-TENANT FEATURE FLAGS
-----------------------------

  Stored in organizations.settings.features (existing JSONB). FEATURE_KEYS
  (superadmin.middleware.js) = { blockchain, verification, qr, aiAnalysis, doki, tracking }
  — booleans; an absent flag means ENABLED (existing tenants unaffected until toggled).
  ('tracking' added 2026-06-28, §49 — gates osint.routes, watermark-scanner.routes, and
  the document tracking toggle.) No schema change.
  Helper isFeatureEnabled / resolveFeatures + middleware requireFeature(key) (403
  FEATURE_DISABLED, fail-open on infra error) applied to: blockchain.routes
  (blockchain), ai-analysis + ai-project routes (aiAnalysis), chatbot routes (doki),
  document verify routes (verification), QR-image route (qr). GET
  /api/organizations/features returns the caller-org flags so the UI hides disabled
  features.

44.3 TENANT TERMINATION (SOFT)
------------------------------

  organizations.isActive (existing) = terminate flag. auth.controller login +
  completeLogin block with 403 TENANT_DISABLED when the user's org isActive=false.
  Reversible: reactivate by setting isActive=true. No cascade delete.

44.4 SUPER-ADMIN API (/api/superadmin/*, requireAdminGate)
----------------------------------------------------------

  GET    /stats        counts+growth, total storage, total AI tokens, request
                       volume/avg-latency/error-rate (apiRequestLogs), service health
                       (db/redis/onlyoffice/vault best-effort probes; redis via ioredis)
  GET    /tenants      list: id, name, ownerEmail, userCount, docCount, storageBytes,
                       aiTokens, isActive, features, createdAt (search + pagination)
  GET    /tenants/:id  aggregate detail (+ newThisMonth)
  POST   /tenants      create org + owner (returns companyCode)
  PATCH  /tenants/:id/status    { isActive } terminate/reactivate
  PATCH  /tenants/:id/features  { features } merge into settings.features

  PRIVACY: tenant views expose AGGREGATE data only — counts, storage, tokens, flags,
  dates, and the OWNER email (the single per-tenant identity intentionally shown).
  Never member names/emails, document contents, folders, or forms. Files:
  controllers/superadmin.controller.js, middlewares/superadmin.middleware.js,
  routes/superadmin.routes.js.

44.5 FRONTEND
-------------

  features/superadmin/SuperAdminDashboard.jsx (password gate → tabs Overview /
  Tenants / Tenant-detail) + services/superadmin.service.js (attaches X-Admin-Gate
  from sessionStorage). Overview: stat cards + performance + service-health pills.
  Tenants: searchable table + Create Tenant modal + per-row terminate/reactivate.
  Tenant detail: aggregate info + 5 feature toggle switches. Dark UI, lucide icons.
  /kicawkicaw routes to it (app/routes/index.jsx).

44.6 VERIFICATION (production)
------------------------------

  node --check on all files + ESM import graph resolves; frontend vite build clean.
  Live: /api/superadmin/stats with gate → 200 with real data (14 users, 10 tenants,
  52 docs); without gate → 401; tenant list shows correct owner emails + per-tenant
  usage; all health probes green (db/redis/onlyoffice/vault). No DB migration needed.

45. TASK DETAIL — INLINE DOCUMENT PREVIEW + EDITOR oo_token FIX (2026-06-20)
================================================================================

  Task detail redesigned: the document is shown INLINE (page images) in the centre
  for ALL roles (filler / reviewer / approver / signer) and ALL statuses, and the
  action container moved to the RIGHT. No separate "View Document" step. Editing
  still happens in the full-screen OnlyOffice editor (filler).

45.1 EDITOR oo_token FIX (root cause of "Container editor tidak ditemukan di DOM")
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  getTaskDocumentConfig built OnlyOffice config WITHOUT a signed token, so the
  editor's server-side /file + /callback fetches (which carry no user Bearer)
  returned 401 → OnlyOffice error -4 → recover path re-init → container lost →
  "Container editor tidak ditemukan di DOM" for the filler role.
  Fix (task.controller.getTaskDocumentConfig): generateOOToken(doc.id) appended to
  both the document url and callbackUrl (?oo_token=...); document key now
  `${docId}_v${versionCount}_${mode}` (dashless, version+mode scoped so a cached
  VIEW session is never reused for an EDIT session); user.id de-dashed. Same pattern
  as the Documents/Forms editors (§39.2).

45.2 BACKEND — AUTHENTICATED PREVIEW ENDPOINTS
----------------------------------------------

  share-preview.service.js: ADDED previewManifest(doc) + previewPage(doc, n) that
  REUSE the existing render pipeline (buildContext + image/pdf/text branches) but
  with NO share watermark and a separate cache namespace (`${docId}:preview:pN`).
  The live share path (renderPage / getManifest) is left byte-for-byte unchanged.
  task.controller.js: getTaskDocumentPreview (manifest) + getTaskDocumentPreviewPage
  (PNG). Auth = assignee OR admin (same rule as getTaskDocumentConfig), org-scoped
  document lookup via shared resolveTaskDoc() helper.
  task.routes.js: GET /api/tasks/:id/document/preview  and  /preview/:n.

45.3 FRONTEND — INLINE PREVIEW + LAYOUT
---------------------------------------

  TaskDocumentPreview.jsx (new): fetches the manifest, then each page as an
  AUTHENTICATED blob (axios responseType:blob → URL.createObjectURL → <img>),
  revealed progressively in a scrollable page-box stack; object URLs revoked on
  unmount/refresh; loading / unsupported / error states.
  task.service.js: getTaskDocumentPreview + getTaskDocumentPreviewPageUrl.
  Tasks.jsx: detail grid → centre (lg:col-span-2) = preview card (doc title + Edit &
  Fill / View Signed buttons + <TaskDocumentPreview>); right (lg:col-span-1) = action
  panel + status badge + Details + Workflow. Redundant review/approve "View Document"
  buttons removed (preview is inline). previewKey state bumps on editor close so the
  inline preview re-fetches the edited document. Container widened to max-w-7xl.

45.4 VERIFICATION
-----------------

  node --check (share-preview, task.controller, task.routes) + frontend vite build
  clean. Live share render path unchanged; OnlyOffice editor flow + action handlers
  (fill/review/approve/sign) intact. No DB migration.

================================================================================
46. PREVIEW FULL-FIDELITY RENDER — POPPLER pdftoppm (2026-06-20)
================================================================================

  Symptom: the SHARE (and new task) preview rendered a PDF as PLAIN, re-flowed TEXT
  — losing headings, tables, colours, header/footer — while the in-app OnlyOffice
  preview showed the real PDF. Reported as "preview ketika share berubah".

46.1 ROOT CAUSE
---------------

  share-preview rendered PDF pages to images via pdfjs-dist + @napi-rs/canvas. On
  Alpine/musl, @napi-rs/canvas throws a native "Value is none of these types
  String/Path/Buffer" while PAINTING GLYPHS, so extractPageAsImage failed and
  share-preview fell back to renderPdfPageAsText (text extracted + laid out plainly).
  The stored blob IS the real PDF (the OnlyOffice /file view proves it) — only the
  image render was failing.

46.2 FIX — NATIVE POPPLER RENDERER
----------------------------------

  ocr.service.js: new renderPdfPageWithPoppler() shells out to poppler `pdftoppm`
  (`-png -r <dpi> -f N -l N -singlefile`) via a temp file — pixel-accurate (same
  engine browsers/Preview use) and stable on Alpine. extractPageAsImage now tries
  poppler FIRST, then falls back to the two pdfjs paths (so local dev without poppler
  still renders). New optional dpi arg (default OCR_RENDER_DPI=300).
  Dockerfile (backend): runtime image adds `poppler-utils`.
  share-preview.service.js: both render calls pass 150 DPI (≈ PREVIEW_WIDTH 1240,
  downscaled by sharp) — crisp, faster, lower memory; OCR keeps 300.

46.3 RESULT
-----------

  Share + task inline preview now match the original PDF exactly (layout, tables,
  colours). The share preview keeps its security watermark tiles (intentional). OCR
  page rendering also more reliable. Requires a backend image REBUILD on next deploy
  (new system dependency); in-memory preview cache clears on container restart.

48. VERIFICATION OVERHAUL — verify-file + on-demand chain + view-document preview (2026-06-28)
================================================================================

  Reworks the "verify a document" surface. Supersedes the wiring described in §30
  (2026-05-24). Backend commit `8e41c72` / `d76f322` ("doc verif"), frontend
  `b253f7d` / `83c9b89`.

48.1 WHAT CHANGED ON THE BACKEND
--------------------------------

  REMOVED endpoints (controller handlers + routes deleted):
    - POST /api/documents/verify-by-hash   (verifyByHash)    — gone
    - POST /api/documents/verify-hardcopy  (verifyByHardcopy) — gone

  NEW / replacement endpoint:
    - POST /api/documents/verify-file  (verifyByFile)
        * authenticate REQUIRED + requireFeature('verification'), multipart `file`.
        * org-scoped: matches only within req.user.organizationId. Rationale in code:
          cross-tenant fuzzy matching would leak other orgs' documents, so the old
          "public anonymous hash lookup" was intentionally dropped.
        * Pipeline: computeUploadDNA → (1) exact SHA-256 via findByContentHash(orgId);
          (2) if no exact match AND no extractable text → friendly not_found (no error);
          (3) else SimHash fuzzy via findBySimHash(orgId, top 5, ssdeep secondary).
        * Verdict from verification.service.decideFileVerdict({exactMatch, similarity}):
            exact            → verified_exact ("Verified (exact original)")
            similarity ≥ 95  → verified_fuzzy ("Verified (registered document)")
            similarity 78-94 → partial        ("Partial match (possibly modified)")
            else             → not_found       ("No matching document")
        * Response document object is MINIMAL: { id, filename, uploader, createdAt,
          version, anchored:boolean }. It does NOT resolve the blockchain here — only
          the `anchored` flag. Logged as inputMethod 'upload_softcopy'.

  NEW endpoint — on-demand chain check (the "file path" needs an explicit button):
    - GET /api/documents/:id/blockchain-integrity  (getBlockchainIntegrity)
        * optionalAuth + requireFeature('verification'); org-scoped when authenticated.
        * Loads latest blockchainAnchors row → resolveBlockchainVerdict (reads the chain,
          not the DB) → returns { state, network, txHash, explorerUrl, blockNumber,
          onChainHash, anchoredAt }.

  NEW endpoint — read-only preview ("View document"):
    - GET /api/documents/:id/verify-preview?page=N  (verifyPreview)
        * optionalAuth, render-only. Calls share-preview.renderVerificationPreview(id,page)
          which renders the matched doc's page to a downscaled PNG (reuses the same
          text/PDF/image/office→PDF pipeline). Streams image/png inline with
          X-Total-Pages header (exposed via Access-Control-Expose-Headers).
        * 415 { previewable:false } for unsupported types. Never returns raw bytes/keys.
        * SECURITY NOTE (from code comment): the underlying DB read is tenant-lint-ignored
          because the preview only reveals a doc the caller already identified via a
          successful verify.

  QR PATHS NOW AUTO-RESOLVE THE CHAIN:
    - verifyByShortCode (GET /verify) and verifyByQrImage (POST /verify-qr-image) now
      attach a full `blockchain` summary onto the returned document
      (buildBlockchainSummary), so the QR flow shows chain status without a second call.
    - buildBlockchainSummary now also carries `blockNumber`, and the not_anchored shape
      returns explicit nulls for network/blockNumber/onChainHash/anchoredAt.

48.2 FRONTEND
-------------

  - services/verification.service.js: verifyByHardcopy() → verifyByFile() (POST
    /documents/verify-file). Added getBlockchainIntegrity(id) and
    getVerifyPreview(id, page) (blob fetch; reads X-Total-Pages; caller revokes blobUrl).
  - features/documents/Verification.jsx heavily rewritten (~1300 lines changed) to match:
    QR short-code, QR image, and digital-file upload; "View document" paginated preview;
    explicit on-demand blockchain-integrity button on the file path.
  - app/routes/index.jsx: NEW public route { path:'/verify', element:<Verification public/> }
    rendered WITHOUT the auth guard (the existing /verification stays authed).

48.3 HONEST CAVEATS
-------------------

  - verify-file requires login by design — there is no longer an anonymous hash check.
  - File verification returns `anchored` only; chain truth requires the separate
    /blockchain-integrity call (one extra round-trip on the file path, by design).
  - §30 below remains in this doc as a historical record of the previous wiring; where
    it conflicts with this section, §48 is authoritative.


================================================================================
49. OSINT TRACKING PIVOT — Google CSE + visible canary codes + QR-on-download (2026-06-28)
================================================================================

  Replaces the previous GitHub-code-search OSINT scanner with a Google Custom Search
  (CSE) discovery flow keyed on a VISIBLE, searchable canary code stamped onto each
  downloaded copy. Backend commit `b16f923` ("update + docs"). DB migrations
  drizzle/0014_osint_tracking.sql and 0015_qr_on_download.sql.

49.1 NEW / REMOVED SERVICES
---------------------------

  REMOVED:  src/services/github-leak-scanner.service.js (runDailyScan, GitHub Code Search).
  NEW:      src/services/tracking-code.service.js   — DLQ canary code gen/parse.
            src/services/google-search.service.js   — Google CSE JSON API wrapper.
            src/services/osint-discovery.service.js — runDiscoveryScan + matchHitsToCodes.
  CHANGED:  leak-scanner.service.js (recordVisibleLeak, stats keyed on trackingEnabled),
            osint.controller.js, index.js (auto-scan cron), document.controller.js,
            document-rewriter.service.js (visible stamp + QR stamp).

49.2 THE CANARY CODE
--------------------

  Format:  DLQ-{docCode}-{dlCode}
    docCode = stable per tracked document, 8 hex (crypto.randomBytes(4)), generated
              ONCE when tracking is first enabled (documents.trackingCode).
    dlCode  = unique per download, 6 chars Crockford base32 (excludes I,L,O,U).
  Full code stored per-download in download_watermarks.visible_code; the searchable
  prefix "DLQ-{docCode}" lets ONE Google query cover every download of a document.
  tracking-code.service.js exports: generateDocCode, generateDownloadCode,
  formatVisibleCode, searchPrefix, parseCodes.

49.3 STAMPING ON DOWNLOAD (document.controller.js)
--------------------------------------------------

  In BOTH download paths — downloadDocumentDecrypted and downloadDocumentConverted —
  when uploadConfig.tracking.enabled AND doc.trackingEnabled AND doc.trackingCode:
    1. dlCode = generateDownloadCode(); visibleCode = formatVisibleCode(...)
    2. stampVisibleCode(buffer, mime, code)  (document-rewriter.service.js):
         - TXT  → appends "ref:DLQ-..." line (method 'text_visible').
         - PDF  → pdf-lib drawText, ~4pt grey 0.6 opacity at (6,4) on every page
                  (method 'pdf_visible').
         - DOCX → jszip edit of word/document.xml: appends a "ref:DLQ-..." <w:p> run
                  (grey 8C8C8C, w:sz = stampSizePt*2 half-points) inserted BEFORE <w:sectPr>
                  so sectPr stays the last child of w:body (method 'docx_visible').
                  OOXML .docx only — legacy .doc, .rtf, .odt, XLSX/PPTX and images stay
                  success:false → served un-stamped, visibleCode set back to null.
                  Config: uploadConfig.tracking.visibleCategories = ['text','pdf','office'].
    3. On success: fire-and-forget INSERT into download_watermarks with visibleCode, the
       stamp method as documentFormat, plus IP/userAgent. Audit log records visibleCode.
    Failure to stamp NEVER blocks the download (served un-stamped, warning logged).

49.4 QR ON DOWNLOAD (separate toggle)
-------------------------------------

  When doc.qrOnDownload is on (and qr feature not disabled): the controller loads the
  active documentQrCodes row, downloads the QR PNG from S3 (qrBucket), and
  stampQrCode(buffer, mime, qrPng, shortCode):
    - PDF  → embeds the QR PNG bottom-right of the LAST page (64px, 18px margin) + a
             tiny short-code caption (working path).
    - DOCX → DEFERRED (returns success:false → served un-stamped).

49.5 DISCOVERY SCAN (osint-discovery.service.js)
------------------------------------------------

  runDiscoveryScan(orgId, { docIds?, maxQueries=90, onLog }):
    - Selects org docs where trackingEnabled=true AND trackingCode not null.
    - Per doc: googleSearch('"DLQ-{docCode}"') → fetch each hit's page text (≤512KB) →
      matchHitsToCodes() de-dupes by URL and keeps codes whose docCode matches THIS doc →
      recordVisibleLeak() per match. 300ms sleep between docs; stops on RATE_LIMITED;
      caps at maxQueries (logs the skipped remainder — NO silent truncation).
    - google-search.service.js: requires GOOGLE_CSE_KEY + GOOGLE_CSE_CX, throws
      GOOGLE_CSE_NOT_CONFIGURED if absent, RATE_LIMITED on HTTP 429.
    - recordVisibleLeak (leak-scanner.service.js): idempotent by
      (documentId, sourceUrl, matchType='visible_code'); inserts a leakScans +
      leakReports row (matchConfidence 95, sourceName 'google_cse'); ATTRIBUTES the leak
      to the exact downloader by looking up download_watermarks.visible_code →
      downloadedBy → leakReports.tracedToUserId.

49.6 STATS / TRACKED LIST / CRON / AUTH
---------------------------------------

  - getMonitorStats + listTrackedDocuments now key the "tracked" set on
    documents.trackingEnabled (was: active honeytokens). autoScanSource = 'google_cse';
    autoScanEnabled requires OSINT_AUTOSCAN_ENABLED='true' AND both Google CSE vars set.
  - checkDocumentOnDemand (POST /osint/documents/:id/check) requires trackingEnabled +
    trackingCode (404 "tracking belum diaktifkan" otherwise), then runs runDiscoveryScan
    for that one doc.
  - index.js auto-scan cron (opt-in, default OFF): 5 min after boot then every 24h, runs
    runDiscoveryScan per org via Google CSE.
  - NEW feature flag key 'tracking' added to FEATURE_KEYS
    (superadmin.middleware.js: blockchain, verification, qr, aiAnalysis, doki, tracking).
    osint.routes and watermark-scanner.routes are gated by requireFeature('tracking').

49.7 NEW TOGGLE ENDPOINTS (document.controller.js + routes)
-----------------------------------------------------------

  - PATCH /api/documents/:id/tracking        body {enabled}  requireFeature('tracking').
      toggleDocumentTracking — authz canManageDocSettings(): same org AND
      (role owner|admin OR user is doc.ownerId). Generates trackingCode on first enable.
  - PATCH /api/documents/:id/qr-on-download   body {enabled}  requireFeature('qr').
      toggleQrOnDownload — same canManageDocSettings authorization.
  Frontend osint.service.js adds toggleTracking(id,enabled) and toggleQrOnDownload(id,enabled).

49.8 NEW ENV VARS + HONEST CAVEATS
----------------------------------

  - GOOGLE_CSE_KEY, GOOGLE_CSE_CX, OSINT_TRACKING_ENABLED, OSINT_AUTOSCAN_ENABLED.
    These are read by code but were NOT added to backend/.env.example as of this commit —

    presentations, images, legacy .doc/.rtf/.odt carry NO visible canary and cannot be
    discovered by this flow — an on-demand check of one reports skippedReason
    'no_visible_code', not 'never_downloaded'.

  - Invisible per-download watermarking (§15) and upload honeytokens (§8.3) still exist
    independently; this section is the VISIBLE, search-indexable layer on top.
  - New unit tests: google-search, honeytoken-roundtrip, osint-discovery-match, stamp-qr,
    toggle-permission, tracking-code, verify-verdict, visible-stamp.

52.B  FEATURE 1 — TAMPER-EVIDENT AUDIT LOG (hash chain + Polygon anchoring)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
GOAL: make audit_logs cryptographically tamper-EVIDENT so any edit/delete/reorder —
including by a DB admin — is detectable. Closes the insider-with-DB-access gap.

  DESIGN — per-organization SHA-256 hash chain:
    entryHash = SHA-256( canonicalSerialization(entry) + prevHash )
    - sequenceNumber: monotonic per organizationId, from 1
    - prevHash: previous entry's entryHash (genesis = 64 zero hex chars)
    - canonical = stable-stringify (sorted keys) of {seq, organizationId, userId, action,
      resourceType, resourceId, details, previousState, newState, ipAddress, userAgent,
      createdAt(ISO-ms)}. createdAt generated app-side (NOT defaultNow) for determinism;
      jsonb fields sorted-key serialized so Postgres key-reordering can't break verify.

  MIGRATION drizzle/0016_audit_hash_chain.sql (additive, backward-compatible):
    - audit_logs +sequence_number(bigint) +prev_hash(text) +entry_hash(text)
      + UNIQUE INDEX audit_org_seq_uq (organization_id, sequence_number)
    - NEW audit_chain_head (organization_id PK, last_seq, last_hash, updated_at) —
      append target + per-org SELECT FOR UPDATE lock to serialize concurrent appends.
    - NEW audit_chain_anchors (id, organization_id, from_seq, to_seq, entry_count,
      root_hash, blockchain_tx_hash, block_number, status, anchored_at).
    Old rows keep NULL chain fields; verifier starts at first non-null sequenceNumber.

  SERVICE backend/src/services/audit.service.js:
    - appendAuditEntry(entry, {tx?}) — db.transaction; SELECT FOR UPDATE on chain head;
      compute seq/prevHash/entryHash; insert row; update head. Optional tx for callers
      already inside a transaction.
    - verifyChain(entries) — PURE: recompute every entryHash + check linkage + seq order.
      Returns {intact, count, brokenAtSeq?, reason?}.
    - verifyChainForOrg(orgId) — loads chained rows in seq order then verifyChain().
    - stableStringify / canonicalAuditEntry / computeEntryHash / GENESIS_HASH.

  ALL 11 db.insert(auditLogs) call sites (6 files: document.controller, folder.controller,
  upload-pipeline, ai-analysis, archive, chatbot) routed through appendAuditEntry. No
  direct auditLogs inserts remain.

  ANCHORING (blockchain.service.js — reuses the contract's batch primitive, does NOT
  write the document blockchainAnchors table):
    - computeAuditRoot(entryHashes) — Merkle root (reuses merkle.util).
    - anchorAuditRoot(root, count) — contract.anchorBatch(root, count), waits confirmations.
    - verifyAuditRoot(root) — contract.verifyBatch(root) → onChain bool.
    backend/src/services/audit-anchor.service.js:
    - anchorOrgPending(orgId, {force}) — anchors entries since last anchored seq;
      force=true anchors any pending, else only when >= AUDIT_ANCHOR_EVERY_N.
    - runAuditAnchoring({force}) — cron tick across all orgs.
    - verifyAnchorsForOrg(orgId) — recompute each anchor's root from CURRENT entries,
      check vs stored root (local) AND on-chain (authoritative) → detects full rewrite.

  CRON (index.js, gated AUDIT_ANCHOR_ENABLED, needs BLOCKCHAIN_ENABLED):
    - check tick every AUDIT_ANCHOR_CHECK_MINUTES → anchors orgs at the EVERY_N threshold
    - flush tick every AUDIT_ANCHOR_INTERVAL_HOURS → anchors leftovers
    Two-tier batching: "every N entries OR every X hours, whichever first." 1 anchor tx
    covers many entries (Merkle root) → ~0.0025 POL/anchor; daily ≈ negligible.
    .env defaults (prod): AUDIT_ANCHOR_ENABLED=true, EVERY_N=256, CHECK_MINUTES=60,
    INTERVAL_HOURS=24. Hash chaining itself is ALWAYS on + free; only anchoring is gated.

  CAVEAT: tamper-EVIDENT, not tamper-PROOF (detects, not prevents). Detection window for a
  full-rewrite attack = the anchoring interval; local edits/deletes are caught instantly on
  verify regardless of anchoring. Amoy testnet immutability < mainnet.

  TWO ANCHOR TYPES (do not confuse):
    - audit anchors  → audit_chain_anchors → shown in /kicawkicaw "Integritas Audit" tab
    - document anchors → blockchainAnchors → shown in /kicawkicaw "Blockchain" tab
