53. SECURITY CAPSTONE — FEATURE 2: HYBRID POST-QUANTUM KEY-WRAPPING (2026-07-01)
================================================================================

GOAL: add a post-quantum-READY layer over envelope encryption by wrapping each
document key (DEK) with a HYBRID KEM — classical X25519 + post-quantum ML-KEM-768
(NIST FIPS 203, lattice / Module-LWE). Mitigates "harvest-now-decrypt-later" (HNDL)
for long-retention documents. Additive + opt-in; existing v1 documents untouched.

WHAT IS / IS NOT:
  - Does NOT replace AES-256-GCM for file content (AES-256 is already quantum-resistant;
    Grover only halves its strength → AES-256 ≈ AES-128 security, still strong).
  - Does NOT replace Vault. Adds ONE wrapping layer on the DEK; the KEM private keys are
    stored wrapped (Vault/master key), same trust model as today.
  - We USE audited libraries (@noble/post-quantum ml_kem768, @noble/curves x25519) — we do
    NOT implement lattice math ourselves.

53.1 KEY HIERARCHY (v2_hybrid_pqc)
----------------------------------
  file bytes
    └─ AES-256-GCM(DEK)                                   ← unchanged
         └─ AES-256-GCM(hybridWrapKey) → wrappedDek       ← NEW
              hybridWrapKey = HKDF-SHA256( ss_x25519 || ss_mlkem )
                ss_mlkem  = ML-KEM-768 encapsulate(org KEM public)
                ss_x25519 = X25519 ECDH(ephemeral priv, org X25519 public)
         (org KEM priv + X25519 priv) wrapped by Vault/master key   ← at rest
  Attacker must break BOTH the classical and the post-quantum part to recover the DEK.
  Hybrid is deliberate: if ML-KEM (new, 2024 standard) is later weakened, security never
  drops below the classical X25519 baseline — and vice-versa.

53.2 DEPENDENCIES (verified available)
  - @noble/post-quantum@0.6.1  → import { ml_kem768 } from '@noble/post-quantum/ml-kem.js'
      keygen() → {publicKey(1184B), secretKey(2400B)}
      encapsulate(pub) → {cipherText(1088B), sharedSecret(32B)};  decapsulate(ct, sec) → ss
  - @noble/curves@2.2.0        → import { x25519 } from '@noble/curves/ed25519.js'
      utils.randomSecretKey(), getPublicKey(priv), getSharedSecret(priv, pub)
  - HKDF + AES-256-GCM + SHA-256: Node crypto.

53.3 FILES
  - backend/src/services/pqc-crypto.js  (PURE, no DB/Vault):
      generateHybridKeypair() → {kemPublicKey, kemSecretKey, x25519Public, x25519Secret} b64
      hybridWrapDek(dek, kemPubB64, x25519PubB64) → envelope
        { mlkemCiphertext, x25519EphemeralPublic, wrapSalt, wrappedDek, wrapIv, wrapTag } b64
      hybridUnwrapDek(envelope, kemSecB64, x25519SecB64) → DEK (throws on tamper/wrong key)
      NOTE: HKDF `info` binds sha256(mlkemCt || ephPub), NOT the raw ciphertext — Node's
      HKDF caps info at 1024 bytes and the ML-KEM ciphertext alone is 1088 bytes.
  - backend/src/services/encryption.service.js:
      + wrapSecret(buf) / unwrapSecret(str) — provider-agnostic (Vault Transit when
        configured, else local AES-256-GCM with master key; unwrap dispatches on "vault:"
        prefix). Used to protect the PQC private keys at rest.
      ~ decryptFile(buf, encKey, iv, authTag, version=null) — 5th arg added. If
        version.keyWrapVersion === 'v2_hybrid_pqc' it LAZY-imports pqc.service and unwraps
        the DEK via the hybrid KEM (lazy import breaks the circular dep); else the v1 path.
  - backend/src/services/pqc.service.js (DB/Vault layer):
      isPqcEnabled() = PQC_WRAP_ENABLED === 'true'
      ensureActiveKeypair(orgId) — lazy: one active pqcKeypairs row/org; privates wrapped
        via wrapSecret. createHybridDocumentKey(orgId) — returns a generateDocumentKey-shaped
        object (+ keyWrapVersion/pqcKeypairId/pqcEnvelope). unwrapHybridDocumentKey(version).

53.4 DATA MODEL (migration drizzle/0018_pqc_hybrid_wrap.sql)
  - document_versions +key_wrap_version text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'v1_vault'
                      +pqc_keypair_id uuid  +pqc_envelope jsonb   (NULL for v1)
  - NEW pqc_keypairs (id, organization_id, kem_public_key, kem_private_key_wrapped,
      x25519_public, x25519_private_wrapped, is_active, created_at).

53.5 UPLOAD / DOWNLOAD
  - Upload (upload-pipeline.service.js): if isPqcEnabled() → createHybridDocumentKey(org),
    else generateDocumentKey() (v1). PQC failure on upload ALWAYS falls back to v1 + a
    warning — uploads never break. Stores key_wrap_version / pqc_keypair_id / pqc_envelope.
  - Download: all 6 decryptFile call sites (document.controller ×3, share-preview,
    ai-analysis, form.controller, signing.controller) now pass the `version` row, so v2
    documents unwrap via the hybrid KEM everywhere; v1 stays on the existing path.

53.6 FEATURE FLAG
  PQC_WRAP_ENABLED — .env.example=false (conservative template); dev .env=true (live).
  Default OFF means nothing changes; ON applies only to NEW uploads (existing docs are v1
  forever and still decrypt). Prod: set true when ready.

53.7 HONEST CAVEATS (state these, don't overclaim)
  - Only the asymmetric KEY-WRAP step is post-quantum; file content is AES-256 (already
    quantum-resistant). NOT "quantum-proof documents".
  - During decapsulation the private keys briefly exist in app memory (Vault protects at
    rest, not during use). Production hardening = KEM ops inside an HSM.
  - PoC-scope readiness, not a full PQC migration. Real near-term value = HNDL mitigation.

53.8 TESTS
  - tests/unit/pqc-wrap.test.js — 7 PURE tests (round-trip; tamper wrappedDek / mlkemCt;
    wrong keypair). Total backend node --test = 140 pass.
  - Service E2E: wrapSecret/unwrapSecret round-trip, v1 regression, v2 round-trip, tamper.
  - FULL pipeline E2E: real uploadPipeline (PQC on) → version stored as v2_hybrid_pqc with
    envelope+keypair → real download path decrypt (hybrid unwrap) → bytes match. Backend
    restarted clean with PQC on.

================================================================================
54. PII REDACTION BEFORE OUTBOUND AI (2026-07-02, commit f753e1d9)
================================================================================

GOAL: narrow structured personal identifiers (email + phone) OUT of document text
BEFORE it crosses the trust boundary toward OpenAI (embedding + chat completion).
Secondary mitigation only — the AI Access Grant (explicit per-document consent) stays
the PRIMARY privacy control; this strips incidental identifiers riding along in
already-consented content. Additive, default ON, never breaks decryption/flow.

54.1 SERVICE — backend/src/services/pii-redaction.service.js (PURE, no DB/network)
  - redactPII(text) → { redactedText, counts:{email,phone} }. Replaces matches with
    the placeholders [EMAIL] / [PHONE]. SCOPE IS DELIBERATE: only email + phone —
    NO names, NO addresses, NO NER.
  - redactForOutbound(text) — flag wrapper. When PII_REDACTION_ENABLED === 'false',
    passes text through untouched with zero regex work (rollback without redeploy).
    Default (unset/anything else) = ON.
  - Mirrors the pqc-crypto.js pattern: pure + isolated-testable.

54.2 REGEX DESIGN (correctness + ReDoS guard)
  - EMAIL_RE: RFC-5321-BOUNDED — local part {1,64}, each domain label {1,63}. Bounded
    quantifiers (not unbounded '+') keep redaction O(n), avoiding O(n^2) catastrophic
    backtracking on pathological input (a long local-part run with no '@').
  - PHONE_ID_RE: Indonesian mobile (+62 / 62 / 0 → 8xx, grouped). PHONE_INTL_RE:
    generic international (leading + country code, grouped).
  - ORDER MATTERS: (1) email BEFORE phone — an email's @domain has digit runs that
    could be mis-read as a phone once stripped; (2) Indonesian phone BEFORE generic
    international — so an ID number isn't partially matched, leaving a mangled residue.
  - IDEMPOTENT: [EMAIL] has no '@', [PHONE] has no digit → re-running never
    re-matches/double-redacts.

54.3 HOOKS — backend/src/services/ai-analysis.service.js
  - grantAIAccess(): redact textContent ONCE, before indexDocument() (→ OpenAI
    embedding → Qdrant upsert). Everything downstream uses redactedText. The
    ai_grant_access audit entry records details.piiRedaction = {email,phone} counts.
  - analyzeDocument(): redact extractedText BEFORE the 30,000-char truncation — cutting
    first could split an email/phone in half and leak an unredacted fragment. redactedText
    then flows through truncation, Qdrant search context, the system prompt, and the
    OpenAI call. Analysis metadata records piiRedaction counts too.

54.4 CONFIG + TESTS
  - .env / .env.example: PII_REDACTION_ENABLED=true (default ON; "false" disables).
  - tests/unit/pii-redaction.test.js — email/phone redaction, ordering, idempotency,
    counts, and a ReDoS/perf case (bounded quantifiers stay linear).

54.5 HONEST CAVEATS
  - Only structured email + phone are removed; free-text names, addresses, ID numbers in
    other formats are NOT (by design — consent is the real control, this is defense-in-depth).
  - OpenAI is still an external processor for consented content; redaction reduces
    incidental exposure, it does not make the flow zero-knowledge (see the E2EE roadmap
    in docloq-tiered-e2ee-future-work-spec.md for that separate, unbuilt tier).

================================================================================
55. QR DECODE + DENSITY FIX, AND ONLYOFFICE TOKEN STABILITY (2026-07-01, commit 17ac021)
================================================================================

(Chronologically this commit precedes §54 (PII, 2026-07-02); documented here for
completeness.) Two viewing/verification robustness fixes.

55.1 QR VERIFICATION — decode failures on clear QR images
  ROOT CAUSE: DocLoq QRs embedded the FULL signed JSON in the image itself —
  { url, sig, payload{docId, 64-hex hash, org, ts, sc} } — ~250+ bytes → a very dense
  QR (high version, ~5px/module at the rendered 300px). jsQR then failed to decode it
  even from the stored PNG at native size, and worse from a screenshot/photo. Symptom:
  "No QR code detected" on a perfectly clear QR.

  FIX A — reduce density (backend/src/services/qrcode.service.js):
    The QR now carries ONLY the short verification URL (…/verify?code=<shortCode>).
    The signature + payload are verified SERVER-SIDE via the shortcode lookup
    (verifyByQrImage uses only decoded.shortCode), so they never needed to live in the
    QR. A ~40-char URL → low QR version → reliably scannable. signature/payload are
    still returned by generateVerificationQR for DB storage.

  FIX B — robust decode (backend/src/services/verification.service.js decodeQrFromBuffer):
    Try native, then an UPSCALE ladder [600, 900, 1200, 1600] (only enlarge), using
    sharp's default lanczos kernel (re-smooths module edges so jsQR's binariser locks
    on) with jsQR inversionAttempts:'attemptBoth' (handles dark-theme/inverted). Do NOT
    grayscale/normalize — that shifts jsQR's own threshold and HURTS decoding (measured).
    Parser now accepts: new plain-URL QR (extract ?code=), legacy JSON QR, or a bare
    shortcode string.

  RESULT: the new low-density URL QR decodes reliably from 80px to 300px (previously
  failed at native 300px). Old dense QRs already generated stay hard — re-upload a
  document to regenerate a low-density QR, or use the manual 8-char code entry.

55.2 ONLYOFFICE_SECRET — stale/partial editor render after a deploy
  ROOT CAUSE: the secret used to sign OnlyOffice's short-lived file-access token was
  `process.env.ONLYOFFICE_SECRET || crypto.randomBytes(32)`. In prod ONLYOFFICE_SECRET
  was UNSET → a random secret per backend PROCESS. Every restart/deploy therefore
  invalidated the tokens of already-open editors → OnlyOffice's fetch of GET /:id/file
  returned 401 → the editor showed stale or partial content (e.g. a large .txt rendering
  as a single truncated page).

  FIX: add ONLYOFFICE_SECRET to .env / .env.example and set a stable value in prod (see
  INSTALLATION-GUIDE-DOCLOQ.txt §A.6 NOTE). With a persistent secret, tokens survive
  restarts and OnlyOffice always re-fetches the full document. (Diagnosis confirmed the
  backend stores + serves the full file — full byte count, BOM-only wrap; the truncation
  was purely the OnlyOffice fetch failing on an invalidated token.)

================================================================================
56. QR IMAGE-VERIFY FIX + LIVE CAMERA QR SCAN (2026-07-12)
================================================================================

56.1 QR IMAGE-VERIFY REJECTED GENUINE CURRENT QRs (backend)
  ROOT CAUSE: §55.1 changed the QR to carry ONLY the verification URL (no embedded
  {payload,sig}). But verifyByQrImage (document.controller.js) still REQUIRED an
  embedded HMAC — decodeQrFromBuffer returns payload=null/signature=null for a URL-only
  QR, so signatureValid became false → every genuine CURRENT QR image was flagged
  "UNSIGNED / possible forgery". Only legacy JSON QRs passed. The camera-scan URL path
  (verifyByShortCode) had NO signature check at all → asymmetric trust between the two.

  FIX (backend/src/controllers/document.controller.js):
    Aligned the image path to the scan path. The 8-char short code is a ~47-bit
    crypto-random token that ONLY DocLoq issues + stores; a decoded short code that
    resolves to a documentQrCodes row therefore proves the QR is DocLoq-issued (same
    trust the scan path already grants; the server is authoritative — unknown code =
    404). verifyByQrImage now defaults signatureValid=true once the short code resolves,
    and only runs the HMAC check when an embedded {payload,sig} IS present (legacy QRs);
    a present-but-invalid HMAC → TAMPERED reject. verifyByShortCode now also returns
    signatureValid:true. Frontend authenticity copy softened (no longer claims "HMAC").
  VERIFIED: decode-shape check (URL-only / legacy / foreign) + unit tests green.

56.2 LIVE CAMERA QR SCAN ON THE VERIFICATION PAGE (frontend, Phase 1)
  Auto-detecting live-camera QR scan added to /verify (phone-only), so a user points the
  camera and it verifies on its own — no photo/upload step. Detection is 100% client-side;
  only the short-code string is sent (same as the scan-URL path). No backend change.
    - Engine (progressive enhancement): native `BarcodeDetector` on Android Chrome; a
      lazily-imported ZXing-C++ WASM ponyfill (npm `barcode-detector@3.2.0`, exposes the
      same W3C interface) everywhere else. iOS Safari has NO native BarcodeDetector, so
      the WASM path is PRIMARY on iPhone. WASM self-hosted via Vite `?url` (no CDN) — build
      emits a fingerprinted zxing_reader.wasm asset + a lazy ponyfill chunk (not in the
      main bundle). jsQR rejected (dormant since 2021).
    - ZERO-FALSE-POSITIVE (4 layers): QR Reed-Solomon (engine emits no result on a garbled
      read) → strict parseShortCode (must be an http(s) URL, path /verify, ?code matching
      ^[A-Za-z0-9]{8}$) → N=2 consecutive-identical temporal consensus → lock + stop the
      camera BEFORE calling verify. Foreign/invalid QRs are ignored silently.
    - PHONE-ONLY gate isPhone(): (pointer:coarse) AND (hover:none) AND (phone UA OR min
      screen side ≤ 500 CSS px). Excludes tablets/iPad and touch laptops. Desktop/tablet →
      no camera, image-upload fallback. Actual availability still gated on getUserMedia.
    - Lifecycle: rear cam (facingMode ideal), track.stop() on lock/unmount/route-change/
      tab-hidden (never leave the camera light on), re-acquire on tab-visible (iOS kills
      backgrounded tracks). Torch on Android only. In-app-webview (WhatsApp/IG) detected →
      "open in Safari/Chrome" notice + upload fallback.
    - UX: framed viewfinder, corner brackets tint indigo→amber(sighted)→emerald(locked),
      motion-safe scan-line, Scan/Upload segmented toggle. prefers-reduced-motion honored.
  FILES: frontend/src/features/documents/qrScan.util.js (parseShortCode + isPhone +
    isInAppBrowser), useQrScanner.js (engine select, consensus, lifecycle), Verification.jsx
    (viewfinder + toggle). Plan: docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-12-live-camera-qr-scan.md.
  STATUS: Phase 1 built + build-verified; live camera needs real-device testing (Android
    Chrome + iOS Safari) — cannot be exercised headlessly.

================================================================================
57. PQC EDIT-DOWNGRADE FIX — HYBRID KEY ON EDIT / ONLYOFFICE SAVE (2026-07-12)
================================================================================

  ROOT CAUSE: §53's hybrid PQC key-wrap ran ONLY on the INITIAL upload. New versions
  created by OnlyOffice edits, PDF→DOCX convert, and form-fill called generateDocumentKey()
  = v1_vault → an edited document's CURRENT version silently reverted to classical-only
  key-wrapping even with PQC enabled. This quietly weakened the F2 (harvest-now-decrypt-
  later) guarantee for any document that was ever edited.

  FIX (backend/src/controllers/document.controller.js):
    New helper deriveNewVersionKey(organizationId): uses createHybridDocumentKey() when
    isPqcEnabled(), inside a try/catch that falls back to generateDocumentKey() — a PQC
    error can NEVER break a save (mirrors the upload pipeline). Wired into BOTH edit-save
    sites (convertToEditable, onlyOfficeCallback); both documentVersions inserts now also
    store keyWrapVersion / pqcKeypairId / pqcEnvelope.

  ONLYOFFICE-SAFE (why it can't break the editor): PQC changes only the server-side
  key-WRAP layer — NOT the file bytes, the AES file cipher, or the OnlyOffice contract.
  OnlyOffice always fetches DECRYPTED bytes via serveDocument, which passes the version row
  to decryptFile; decryptFile dispatches on version.keyWrapVersion === 'v2_hybrid_pqc' and
  unwraps the hybrid envelope. All read paths (serve/download/download-as/AI/forms/signing/
  share-preview) pass the version row, so a v2-wrapped edited version decrypts everywhere.
  The only new failure surface (createHybridDocumentKey = DB read + Vault wrap) is caught
  → worst case v1, exactly today's behavior.
  VERIFIED: pqc-wrap.test.js 7/7 + real-document encrypt→wrap→unwrap→decrypt round-trip
  green; all decryptFile callers confirmed to pass the version row.

================================================================================
58. SUPERADMIN OPERATIONAL DASHBOARDS — 5 PRIVACY-SAFE FEATURES (2026-07-12)
================================================================================

  Five aggregate-only operational features added to /kicawkicaw. HARD PRIVACY RULE on
  every query: COUNTs / rates / max-timestamps / config enums only — no document content,
  no filenames/short-codes/content-hashes, no member emails (beyond the existing owner
  email), no verification requester/IP. Per-tenant security counts use k=5 suppression.
  Backend: superadmin.controller.js + superadmin.routes.js (all under requireAdminGate).
  Frontend: superadmin.service.js + SuperAdminDashboard.jsx.

  1) BLOCKCHAIN HEALTH — GET /superadmin/blockchain/health. Wallet POL balance +
     lowBalance vs POLYGON_MIN_BALANCE (new env, default 0.5), pending doc anchors, audit-
     anchor backlog (sum over orgs of head.lastSeq − max anchored toSeq), last-anchor age.
     Warns before anchoring silently dies on empty gas (protects the F1 guarantee). UI:
     amber banner + 3 tiles in the Blockchain tab.
  2) PQC / ENCRYPTION ADOPTION — GET /superadmin/encryption. Platform + per-tenant split of
     documentVersions by keyWrapVersion (v2_hybrid_pqc vs v1_vault), % v2, orgs with an
     active pqcKeypair. Surfaces the F2 rollout (and the §57 fix). UI: Keamanan tab.
  3) SELF-AUDIT TRAIL — GET /superadmin/admin-activity. The most-privileged surface now
     records its own actions. logAdminAction writes into the target org's tamper-evident
     chain from createTenant / setTenantStatus / setTenantFeatures. UI: "Aktivitas Admin"
     tab. GOTCHA: audit_logs.action is the fixed `audit_action` enum — admin actions use a
     VALID enum value (create/update) + the specific verb in details.adminAction + a
     details.actor='superadmin_gate' marker; the read endpoint filters on the marker, NOT
     on action (same convention as ai_grant_access). Best-effort: an audit failure never
     breaks the mutation.
  4) PLATFORM SECURITY DASHBOARD — GET /superadmin/security. TOTP adoption %, currently-
     locked accounts, failed-login sum, unresolved securityEvents by type (30d), unverified-
     email %, Google-SSO vs password split. Platform + per-tenant (k=5 suppressed). UI:
     Keamanan tab.
  5) TENANT HEALTH — aggregatesFor/toTenant enriched with lastActiveAt (MAX lastLoginAt),
     dormant flag (>30d), activeUsers, AI quota (used/limit), retention days. UI: "Terakhir
     aktif" column + dormant badge in the Tenants tab.

  ADVERSARIAL REVIEW (subagent) caught + FIXED two runtime bugs before ship: (a) the
  audit-enum issue above (would 500 every /admin-activity call + drop all admin audit
  writes); (b) a null-organizationId group key ("null" string) crashing the uuid inArray in
  /security and /encryption → filtered out. NOTE: the superadmin gate is still a single
  static ADMIN_GATE_SECRET (no per-admin identity / MFA / rate-limit) — hardening deferred.
  Plan: docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-12-superadmin-5-features.md.

60. SMART-CONTRACT DEPLOY VIA ETHERS.JS (NO HARDHAT) (2026-07-13)
================================================================================

  DECISION: the DocLoqAnchor contract is now DEPLOYED with ethers.js v6 — the same
  library the runtime blockchain.service.js already uses — NOT hardhat.

  WHY it works without hardhat: the contract is already compiled; its ABI + bytecode
  live in backend/contracts/DocLoqAnchor.json (committed). Deployment only needs a
  bytecode + a funded signer, both of which ethers provides. Hardhat is retained ONLY
  as an optional compiler/test-runner for DocLoqAnchor.sol; it is no longer on the
  deploy path.

  NEW: backend/scripts/deploy-ethers.cjs
    - loads backend/.env (dotenv) → POLYGON_RPC_URL / POLYGON_CHAIN_ID / POLYGON_PRIVATE_KEY
    - reads abi+bytecode from contracts/DocLoqAnchor.json
    - new ethers.JsonRpcProvider(rpc, chainId) + ethers.Wallet(pk, provider)
    - new ethers.ContractFactory(abi, bytecode, wallet) → factory.deploy() →
      waitForDeployment() → getAddress()
    - contract.authorizeAnchor(wallet.address) (backend uses the same wallet)
    - writes the deployed address back into contracts/DocLoqAnchor.json
    - prints the .env vars to set (BLOCKCHAIN_ENABLED, POLYGON_*).

  RUN: cd backend && node scripts/deploy-ethers.cjs
  (Recompile only if DocLoqAnchor.sol changes: `npx hardhat compile` — deployment
  still uses ethers.) Verified the ethers ContractFactory builds a valid deploy
  transaction from the committed artifact offline (dry-run, no gas). Legacy
  scripts/deploy.cjs (hardhat) is kept but superseded.

================================================================================
61. OSINT DISCOVERY — MULTI-PROVIDER SEARCH, HARDENING, SCAN OPTIMIZATION,
    ADMIN LEAK MONITOR (2026-07-12..13)
================================================================================

61.1 DISCOVERY HARDENING (verify-flow fixes)
  - Error surfacing: runDiscoveryScan no longer swallows search failures — returns
    { scanned, queries, confirmed, hits, matches, searchError, searchUnavailable, scanId }.
    osint.controller maps error codes → searchErrorMessage. A misconfigured search
    provider now shows "search unavailable" in the UI instead of a misleading "0 leaks".
  - SSRF: fetchHitText (osint-discovery.service.js) now uses safeFetch (ssrf-guard.js,
    validates every redirect hop via assertUrlPublic) instead of raw fetch — a
    third-party result page can no longer redirect the server to an internal host.
    8s AbortController timeouts on both googleSearch and fetchHitText.
  - Frontend (OSINTTracker.jsx): fixed the Check-Document panel (was reading
    .hits/.scanId that the API never sent → always "No leaks found"); rewrote the
    stale "GitHub Code Search"/honeytoken-hash copy to the Google-CSE + visible-code
    model; added a "visible_code" leak badge.

61.2 MULTI-PROVIDER SEARCH + SERPER FALLBACK
  - Provider abstraction: backend/src/services/search-provider.js `searchWeb(query)`
    tries providers in OSINT_SEARCH_PROVIDERS order (default "google_cse,serper");
    first success wins; unconfigured/errored → next. So a Google CSE 403 automatically
    falls back to Serper.
  - Providers: google-search.service.js (unchanged) + NEW serper-search.service.js
    (POST google.serper.dev/search, X-API-KEY header, organic[] → {url,title,snippet},
    num capped at 10 = 1 credit/query, timeout + error codes SERPER_NOT_CONFIGURED/
    RATE_LIMITED/SERPER_HTTP_x/SERPER_TIMEOUT). Serper free tier = 2500 queries, no card.
  - recordVisibleLeak takes a `source` param → the leak's sourceName reflects the
    real provider (serper/google_cse), not a hardcoded value.
  - Env: OSINT_SEARCH_PROVIDERS, SERPER_API_KEY, SERPER_MONTHLY_BUDGET in .env.example.
  - Verified with a live Serper call (real results + full match pipeline).

61.3 SCAN OPTIMIZATION (frugality — plan docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-13-osint-scan-optimization.md)
  - #1 Skip docs never downloaded WITH a visible stamp: the tracked-doc select adds
    EXISTS(download_watermarks with non-null visible_code). A doc that was never
    downloaded cannot be on the web → not queried. On-demand check of such a doc →
    { skippedReason } (see §61.4 for how the reason is resolved).
  - #2 OR-batch docCodes into one query ("DLQ-a" OR "DLQ-b" …, BATCH_SIZE=15) →
    ⌈N/15⌉ credits instead of N. fetchHitText deduped per URL; matchHitsToCodes
    re-run per docCode over the shared hit set so attribution stays per-code.
  - #4 One leak_scans summary row per scan (even 0 hits): status running→completed,
    searchQueries/sourcesSearched recorded; the scanId is threaded into
    recordVisibleLeak so hits reuse it (no per-hit scan rows, no double-count). This
    makes getMonitorStats.lastScanAt update after clean scans.
  - #5 getMonitorStats.autoScanEnabled/autoScanSource derive from
    configuredProvidersWithKeys() → reflect the REAL provider (serper), not a
    hardcoded google_cse.
  - Verified live (real Postgres + Serper, 2 credits): #1 excludes undownloaded doc,
    #2 → queries=1 for N docs, #4 → one reused scan row + credit delta=1, attribution
    reuses scanId. No bug.

61.4 ADMIN LEAK MONITOR (privacy-safe)
  - GET /api/superadmin/leak-monitoring — AGGREGATE + operational only: platform
    counts (tracked docs, coded downloads, scans 7d/total, leaks 30d/total, last scan),
    provider status (configured + autoscan), Serper credit usage (=
    SUM(jsonb_array_length(search_queries)) over serper scans) vs SERPER_MONTHLY_BUDGET
    (default 2500), per-tenant activity counts, and recent-scan metadata. NEVER exposes
    leak URLs, document names, or which user leaked what.
  - UI: new "Leak Monitor" tab in the /kicawkicaw CRT dashboard (credit used/budget
    bar, stat tiles, per-tenant table, recent-scans table).

61.5 PRIVACY NOTE (search-provider query exposure)
  - Only the "DLQ-<docCode>" token is sent to the search provider — never document
    content, PII, the full code, or the file. Query privacy on Serper = the dashboard's
    "Anonymize queries" toggle (operator setting). Optimization #1 also reduces how many
    codes are ever sent. Stronger options (future): Brave Search API (privacy-first,
    doesn't retain queries) or a self-hosted Common Crawl index (no query leaves infra).

61.6 DOCX VISIBLE CANARY + HONEST SKIP REASON (2026-07-17)
  Bug: Check Document on a tracked .docx reported "belum pernah diunduh" even after the
  doc HAD been downloaded. Two causes, both fixed:
  - Cause A — DOCX carried no canary. stampVisibleCode returned success:false for the
    'office' category ("Phase C"), so no download_watermarks row with visible_code was
    ever written, so the scan's EXISTS(visible_code IS NOT NULL) gate excluded the doc.
    Fix: stampDocxVisible() (see §49.2) — OOXML .docx now stamps like TXT/PDF. Both
    download paths (decrypted + converted) inherit it; the stamp is applied AFTER the
    invisible watermark and both survive together (covered by a chained test).
  - Cause B — the message lied. tracked.length===0 on an on-demand check always mapped to
    'never_downloaded'. Fix: skipReasonFor({isOnDemand, downloadCount, visibleCount}) in
    osint-discovery.service.js resolves it against an actual download_watermarks count:
      downloadCount > 0 → 'no_visible_code'  (downloaded, but nothing searchable stamped)
      downloadCount = 0 → 'never_downloaded'
      not on-demand     → 'no_tracked_docs'
    Both reasons short-circuit before any provider call (0 search credits spent).
    OSINTTracker.jsx renders a distinct message for 'no_visible_code'.
  Not retroactive: downloads taken BEFORE this fix have no visible_code, so an untouched
  doc still reports 'no_visible_code' until it is downloaded again. Formats with no
  carrier (XLSX, PPTX, images, legacy .doc/.rtf/.odt) permanently report 'no_visible_code'.
  Tests: tests/unit/visible-stamp.test.js (DOCX stamp, sectPr placement, repeat download,
  chained invisible+visible, .doc/spreadsheet/PNG still unsupported),
  tests/unit/osint-skip-reason.test.js (reason matrix).

62. AI PROJECTS — STUDIO OUTPUTS, IMAGE GENERATION, EXPANDED SOURCES (current state) (2026-07-16..18)
================================================================================

Supersedes the Section 28 / Section 31 description of the AI Projects workspace;
this section reflects the CURRENT implementation. Additive / backward-compatible:
existing projects, sources, and chat threads keep working unchanged.

62.1 SOURCE TYPES (max 10 per project)
--------------------------------------
  Service: src/services/ai-project.service.js
  A project can mix FIVE source kinds (all chunked + embedded into Qdrant; each
  ingests as an async job: status processing -> active/failed):
    - 'document'  addDocumentSource() — an uploaded doc WITH AI access granted
                  (documents.aiAccessGranted = true). Consent enforced, else
                  RequiresConsentError.
    - 'url'       addUrlSource()      — article URL, fetched via the SSRF-guarded
                  url-ingest.service.js (capped by AI_PROJECT_URL_MAX_BYTES).
    - 'youtube'   addYoutubeSource()  — YouTube URL; transcript pulled via
                  youtube-ingest.service.js.
    - 'text'      addTextSource()     — raw pasted text.
    - 'note'      a saved project note promoted to a source (sourceType 'note').
  (Sections 28/31 documented only 'document' + 'url'.)

62.2 CHAT + CITATIONS
---------------------
  Service: src/services/ai-project-chat.service.js
  - Answers cite their sources inline as [1], [2] (CitationMarker). Clicking a
    citation scrolls to that SOURCE card and shows the quoted passage/page in a
    popover — it does NOT jump to an exact character offset inside the document.
  - The project's custom "AI Instructions" are injected into the system prompt on
    every answer (and every Studio generation).
  - NOT cached: project chat always calls the model. The result cache in
    ai-cache.service.js belongs to the separate AI Document Analysis feature (§7),
    NOT to project chat. (Corrects the "answers are cached" claim in §28.)
  - Quota: AI_PROJECT_CHAT_LIMIT_24H messages per project per 24h (default 50).

62.3 STUDIO OUTPUTS
-------------------
  Services: src/services/ai-project-output.service.js, output-schemas.js
  The Studio panel turns the project's sources into structured, saved outputs.
  Grid-exposed kinds (output-schemas.js STUDIO_KINDS):
      report, slides, mindmap, infographic
  Each kind has a strict JSON schema (SPECS) the model must satisfy. Additional
  schemas (faq, timeline, datatable, compliance) exist in OUTPUT_KINDS for API use
  but are intentionally NOT surfaced in the Studio grid. Generated outputs are
  persisted (ai_project_outputs table) and re-openable.
  (Replaces the old "Quick Actions: summary / timeline / FAQ" description.)

62.4 AI IMAGE OUTPUTS (encrypted, crypto-shreddable)
----------------------------------------------------
  Service: src/services/ai-image.service.js
  - Model: env AI_IMAGE_MODEL, default gpt-image-2 (ai-image.service.js:11 — the
    file header comment still says gpt-image-1). Sizes 1024x1024 / 1536x1024 (landscape)
    / 1024x1536 (portrait).
  - The PNG is encrypted under the PROJECT DEK (AES-256-GCM) and stored in R2 at
    ai-outputs/{projectId}/{outputId}.png.enc — treated as content, so it is erased
    by the project's crypto-shred, exactly like a document blob.
  - Certain Studio outputs (e.g. report / infographic covers) embed a generated
    image.

62.5 NOTES / INSTRUCTIONS / INSIGHT (UI)
----------------------------------------
  Frontend: frontend/src/features/ai-projects (StudioPanel.jsx, ChatPanel.jsx,
  StudioSettingsModal)
  The earlier "Notes / Instructions / Insight" side TABS were removed. Now: Notes
  live in the outputs/side list, and Instructions + Insight open from the settings
  (gear) icon. (Corrects the "Side tabs: Notes, Instructions, Insight" text in §28.)

62.6 LIMITS (unchanged values)
------------------------------
  AI_PROJECT_MAX_PER_ORG    = 20 projects / org
  AI_PROJECT_MAX_SOURCES    = 10 sources / project
  AI_PROJECT_CHAT_LIMIT_24H = 50 messages / project / 24h

62.7 DOC RECONCILIATION
-----------------------
  The in-app docs (frontend/src/features/docs/content.js) and the user manuals
  (manual-guide-en.txt / manual-guide-id.txt) were reconciled to this behavior on
  2026-07-18: removed the stale "answers are cached" claim, the removed side-tabs,
  and the old "summary / timeline / FAQ" quick actions; added Studio outputs, AI
  image outputs, and the text / YouTube source types.

63. SELF-SERVICE PASSWORD RESET / CHANGE + EMAIL REDESIGN + AUTH RATE LIMITING (2026-07-18)
================================================================================

Closes three doc↔repo gaps: the login "Forgot password?" link was a dead route,
the Settings "Change Password" form was inert, and the recovery endpoints had no
rate limiting. All additive — NO schema migration (the password_reset_tokens table
has existed since migration 0000_goofy_magma.sql).

63.1 ENDPOINTS
--------------
  Routes: backend/src/routes/auth.routes.js   Controller: auth.controller.js
    POST /api/auth/forgot-password   (public, rate-limited)  forgotPassword
    POST /api/auth/reset-password    (public, rate-limited)  resetPassword
    POST /api/auth/change-password   (auth,   rate-limited)  changePassword

63.2 FORGOT / RESET FLOW
------------------------
  - forgotPassword ALWAYS returns a generic 200 ("if that email is registered, a
    reset link has been sent") so the response cannot be used to enumerate accounts.
    Optional Cloudflare Turnstile is verified when a captcha token is supplied.
  - A raw 32-byte token is emailed inside a link (APP_URL/reset-password?token=…);
    only the SHA-256 HASH of the token is stored in password_reset_tokens (token
    TEXT, expiresAt, usedAt) — a DB leak yields no usable link. Expiry:
    PASSWORD_RESET_EXPIRY_MINUTES = 30. One live token per user (prior unused tokens
    are deleted on each request).
  - resetPassword is single-use (usedAt IS NULL + not expired), re-hashes the
    password (bcrypt, 12 rounds), clears failedLoginAttempts / lockedUntil, marks
    the token used, and DELETES all userSessions for that user (forced re-login
    everywhere).

63.3 CHANGE PASSWORD (authenticated)
------------------------------------
  changePassword verifies the current password, rejects new == current, enforces
  authConfig.password.minLength (8), re-hashes, and signs out every OTHER device
  (keeps the caller's current session). Frontend: the Settings → Security form is
  now wired (previously inert) — Settings.jsx handleChangePassword +
  authService.changePassword.

63.4 RATE LIMITING (NEW)
------------------------
  Middleware: backend/src/middlewares/rate-limit.middleware.js (express-rate-limit,
  in-memory store; app has `trust proxy` = 1 so req.ip is the real client IP).
  Per-IP limits over a shared window:
    AUTH_FORGOT_LIMIT          default 5
    AUTH_RESET_LIMIT           default 10
    AUTH_CHANGE_PASSWORD_LIMIT default 10
    AUTH_RECOVERY_WINDOW_MIN   default 15 (minutes)
  Over-limit → HTTP 429 with a generic "too many attempts" message.

63.5 EMAIL SERVICE REDESIGN
---------------------------
  Service: backend/src/services/email.service.js
  - New sender sendPasswordResetEmail(email, resetUrl, userName) and exported
    PASSWORD_RESET_EXPIRY_MINUTES. sendOTPEmail is retained (still used by
    totp.controller.js email-OTP and ssh-console.service.js).
  - Both emails now share ONE dark, typographic shell (renderShell): near-black
    #08080a surface, hairline borders, letter-spaced labels, monospace codes, a
    bulletproof table CTA button, and hidden preheader text. NO emoji and NO SVG
    (both are stripped/inconsistent in Gmail/Outlook) and no decorative icon glyphs
    (the old table-drawn lock and coloured alert boxes were removed).
  - Footer links are data-driven: "Help Center" -> APP_URL/contact by default;
    "Privacy" / "Terms" render ONLY when EMAIL_PRIVACY_URL / EMAIL_TERMS_URL are
    set, so the footer never contains a dead link. Overrides: EMAIL_HELP_URL,
    EMAIL_PRIVACY_URL, EMAIL_TERMS_URL, APP_URL.
  - renderOtpEmailHtml / renderPasswordResetEmailHtml are exported for preview and
    testing (build the HTML without sending).

63.6 FRONTEND
-------------
  New pages: frontend/src/features/auth/ForgotPassword.jsx (with Turnstile) and
  ResetPassword.jsx (reads ?token=, sets the new password). New routes
  /forgot-password and /reset-password (app/routes/index.jsx) — the login page's
  "Forgot password?" link now resolves instead of hitting the 404 catch-all. i18n
  keys added to i18n/auth.js (auth.forgot.*, auth.reset.*) and i18n/settings.js
  (settings.security.pw*) in both ID and EN.

63.7 ENV VARS ADDED (all optional, safe defaults)
-------------------------------------------------
  APP_URL, EMAIL_HELP_URL, EMAIL_PRIVACY_URL, EMAIL_TERMS_URL, AUTH_FORGOT_LIMIT,
  AUTH_RESET_LIMIT, AUTH_CHANGE_PASSWORD_LIMIT, AUTH_RECOVERY_WINDOW_MIN. See §3.

64. VPS / SSH ACCESS AUDIT (SUPER-ADMIN) (2026-07-26)
================================================================================

  Every step of the web-SSH console flow is now audited, not just the WebSocket
  session. Metadata only — keystrokes, terminal output and passwords are never
  recorded.

64.1 EVENTS
-----------
  Emitted by ssh-console.service.js auditSsh(). Severity is fixed per type in
  SSH_EVENT_SEVERITY; an unknown type falls back to 'high'.

    ssh_otp_sent        medium    OTP mailed to the fixed operator inbox
    ssh_otp_failed      medium    not-configured / cooldown / rate / send-error
    ssh_ticket_issued   high      gate + OTP passed, a shell can now be opened
    ssh_ticket_denied   high      wrong or expired OTP at ticket issuance
    ssh_console_open    high      shell established on the VPS (user@host logged)
    ssh_console_close   medium    session ended (reason logged)
    ssh_console_denied  high      WS upgrade with bad/expired/foreign-IP ticket
    ssh_access_locked   critical  request arrived while the IP was locked out

  The first four are new HTTP-layer call sites in superadmin.controller.js
  (sendSshOtp, createSshTicket); the last four already existed in
  ws/ssh-console.ws.js and now reach both destinations below.

64.2 TWO DESTINATIONS
---------------------
  auditSsh() builds one payload (buildSshAuditPayload — pure, unit-tested) and
  writes it twice, each best-effort:

    security_events  unchanged shape; ops alerting. organizationId/userId NULL,
                     ip lifted to the ipAddress column.
    audit_logs       NEW. resource_type='system', action='update' (the column is
                     a fixed enum — the real verb is details.adminAction), and
                     details.actor='superadmin_gate' so the super-admin Admin
                     Activity tab picks it up. userAgent goes to its own column.

  auditSsh() can neither throw nor reject, and callers do not await it: an audit
  failure must never delay or block operator access.

64.3 UI
-------
  GET /api/superadmin/admin-activity now matches resource_type IN
  ('organization','system') instead of 'organization' only, and returns
  resourceType. AdminActivityTab renders 'system' rows with a Server icon and the
  "Platform (VPS)" scope label, tints the action badge by details.severity, and
  summarises ssh_* rows as "IP x · user@host · reason: … · stage: …".
  i18n keys: superadmin.adminActivity.actionSsh*, scopePlatform, reasonLabel,
  stageLabel (ID + EN).

64.4 LIMITATION — NOT TAMPER-EVIDENT
------------------------------------
  These rows are NOT hash-chained and NOT blockchain-anchored. The chain in §F1 is
  per-organization (audit_chain_head.organization_id is the PK and the lock row);
  a VPS event has no tenant, so appendAuditEntry() takes its organizationId-null
  branch and stores the row with sequence_number / prev_hash / entry_hash all
  NULL. Merkle anchoring only collects rows that have a sequence_number, so these
  are skipped there too.

  Consequence: SSH audit rows can be deleted or edited in the database without
  breaking any verification. They are an operator trail, not tamper-evident
  evidence. Closing this needs a separate platform-scoped chain (own table + head
  + anchor rows) or a reserved platform organization — neither is implemented.

64.5 OTHER
----------
  frontend/eslint.config.js: no-empty now uses { allowEmptyCatch: true }. The
  changed files carry no comments (deliberate — comments were stripped on
  request), which turned `catch { /* ignore */ }` into an empty block that the
  default rule rejects.

================================================================================
65. AUDIT INTEGRITY TAB — PROOF, NOT JUST COUNTERS (2026-07-26)
================================================================================

  The super-admin Audit Integrity tab answered "how many"; it now also answers
  "prove it" and "is anchoring even on".

65.1 ENTRY PROOF INSPECTOR
--------------------------
  New GET /api/superadmin/audit/entry-proof?orgId=&seq= backed by the pure
  buildEntryProof() in audit.service.js. Returns the full recomputation of one
  entry: canonical sha256 + byte length, stored prevHash, expected prevHash (the
  previous row's entryHash, or the all-zero genesis), recomputed entryHash,
  stored entryHash, and the two verdicts prevLinkOk / hashOk.

  PRIVACY: the tab must never show tenant content (listAuditEntries already drops
  `details` for this reason). So the proof returns, per hashed field, only a
  sha256 digest plus a present/redacted flag. Raw values are returned ONLY for
  seq / organizationId / action / resourceType / createdAt (PROOF_PUBLIC_FIELDS)
  — all of which the tab already displays. userId, resourceId, details,
  previousState, newState, ipAddress and userAgent are digest-only. A digest is
  still sufficient to show the field is locked into the hash.

  canonicalAuditEntry() was split into canonicalAuditFields() + stableStringify()
  so the proof and the hash read the same field list. Key sorting is unchanged,
  so the hash output is byte-identical — no chain re-baseline.

  UI: HashChainViz nodes and the log-entry rows are now buttons; either opens
  EntryProofDrawer (Escape / backdrop to close). The drawer shows the 6 numbered
  computation steps with match/mismatch verdicts, then the per-field digest table
  and the privacy note.

65.2 THIRD-PARTY VERIFICATION BLOCK
-----------------------------------
  chain-status now also returns getChainMeta() from blockchain.service.js:
  chainId, network, explorerBase, contractAddress. The Merkle panel renders the
  FULL merkle root and contract address with copy buttons, plus three steps to
  verify independently: copy root -> open contract on the explorer -> call
  verifyBatch(root) and expect exists = true. The hardcoded AUDIT_EXPLORER
  constant is now only a fallback; tx links use explorerBase, so switching to
  Polygon mainnet no longer mislinks to the Amoy explorer.

65.3 HONEST ANCHORING STATUS
----------------------------
  chain-status returns anchoring { enabled, everyN, checkMinutes, intervalHours }
  read from AUDIT_ANCHOR_*. When anchoring is off (or the wallet is not ready)
  the tab shows an explicit warning: the hash chain still catches partial edits,
  deletions and insertions, but a rewrite of the ENTIRE audit table would go
  undetected — plus which env var caused it. The coverage panel also shows the
  schedule and how many more entries are needed for the next threshold.

65.4 OTHER
----------
  i18n superadmin.audit.* gained the proof/verify/anchor-off/schedule keys
  (ID + EN). Tests: 5 new cases in tests/unit/audit-chain.test.js cover the clean
  proof, the genesis row, a tampered row, a broken prevHash, and digest stability
  under jsonb key reordering — including an assertion that the private fields are
  never returned. Suite 392/392.

  All files touched in this change carry no comments, per the same standing
  request noted in §64.5.

================================================================================
66. AUDIT INTEGRITY HARDENING — FALSE POSITIVES, MERKLE PROOFS, FORENSICS
    (2026-07-28)
================================================================================

  §52.B built the hash chain and §65 built the proof inspector. This pass audited
  both against the code, found the chain could be defeated or falsely alarmed in
  several ways, and closed them. It also makes the Merkle layer actually usable
  instead of a checksum.

66.A  FALSE POSITIVES IN THE HASH FUNCTION (pre-existing, highest impact)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  entryHash is computed at append time from the INPUT object, but verifyChain
  recomputes it from the row READ BACK out of Postgres. Any column that does not
  round-trip byte-identically therefore reports "entryHash mismatch (this row was
  tampered)" permanently, on healthy data. Three reachable cases existed:

    - undefined inside a jsonb field. stableStringify emits "parentId":null;
      drizzle's jsonb mapper is JSON.stringify, which DROPS the key. Triggered by
      folder.controller.js whenever a client omits parentId on a root folder.
    - a Date inside a jsonb field. stableStringify renders it {} (no own keys);
      jsonb stores the ISO string.
    - an UPPERCASE uuid in resourceId. validate.middleware.js accepts uuids
      case-insensitively and does not normalise, but Postgres uuid_out always
      returns lowercase. Any authenticated user could poison their own org's
      chain permanently just by requesting /api/documents/<UPPERCASE-UUID>/download.

  FIX — canonicalAuditFields() now normalises to what Postgres will return:
    jsonbNorm(v) = JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(v))   -> drops undefined, ISO-ifies Date
    uuidNorm(v)  = v.toLowerCase()                 -> organizationId, userId, resourceId

  BACKWARD COMPATIBILITY: both are a no-op for ordinary values, so existing healthy
  rows hash identically and no re-baseline is needed. tests/unit/audit-canonical-
  roundtrip.test.js pins the exact canonical string of a plain entry so a future
  edit cannot silently move the hash.

  NOT RECOVERABLE: rows already poisoned by these three bugs keep a stored
  entryHash computed from the wrong value. The fix prevents new damage; it cannot
  heal old rows.

66.B  TAIL TRUNCATION — THE CHAIN'S BLIND SPOT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  verifyChain only ever saw rows that still EXIST. Deleting the NEWEST rows leaves
  a chain perfectly consistent with itself, so it reported intact. audit_chain_head
  records the last seq/hash ever appended and a plain DELETE does not rewrite it,
  which makes it the witness.

    checkChainHead(entries, head) -> { checked, ok, headMissing, expectedSeq,
                                       actualSeq, expectedHash, actualHash,
                                       missingTail }
    verifyChain(entries, head)    -> head is OPTIONAL. Omitting it skips the check
                                     (all pre-existing single-argument callers are
                                     unaffected). Passing explicit null asserts the
                                     head row is GONE, which is itself a failure
                                     when chained rows exist — otherwise deleting
                                     one head row would disarm the whole feature.

  Also caught by the head hash: a last row rewritten WITH a correctly recomputed
  entryHash. The row scan is satisfied by that; only head.lastHash exposes it.

  brokenAtSeq is null on every head-mismatch path — there is no offending row to
  point at, and the old code returned the last GOOD seq, which pointed auditors at
  an intact row. Activity.jsx rendered "broken at #null" for a fully wiped log.

  RACE (would have been a new bug): verifyChainForOrg reads audit_logs and
  audit_chain_head. Under the default READ COMMITTED each statement gets its own
  snapshot, so an append landing between them leaves head ahead of the rows and
  reports a healthy chain as truncated — on a page that auto-verifies on every
  load, in a system that writes an audit row on nearly every action. Both reads
  now share one snapshot:
      db.transaction(fn, { isolationLevel: 'repeatable read', accessMode: 'read only' })
  A read-only REPEATABLE READ transaction cannot serialisation-fail on a primary.

66.C  VERIFICATION IS OWNER-ONLY — DOCUMENTATION CORRECTION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  This section corrects a doc/code drift, not a code change. The code was ALREADY
  owner-only; the documentation still described it as public in ten places and even
  supplied a rationale for it (old DOC-004).

  CURRENT REALITY — backend/src/routes/document.routes.js, every path guarded by
  authenticate + authorize(['owner']) + requireFeature('verification'):
      GET  /api/documents/verify                      (short code)
      POST /api/documents/verify-qr-image             (uploaded QR PNG/JPG)
      POST /api/documents/verify-file                 (uploaded file, SHA-256 + SimHash)
      GET  /api/documents/:id/verify-preview
      GET  /api/documents/:id/blockchain-integrity
  Frontend: routes/index.jsx wraps /verify in <RequirePermission roles="owner">.
  A QR scan by a non-owner, or a direct API call, is rejected.

  GET /api/documents/verify-by-hash no longer exists at all (removed in §48). The
  frontend verification.service.js still defines verifyByHash() calling it — dead
  code, unused by the UI.

  Corrected in this pass: §10 endpoint table + note, §11 frontend route table,
  DOC-004, §21.3 router-guard note, §30 flow diagram, §30.9 security notes, §42
  capability matrix, §5/§8 QR short-code descriptions.

66.D  MERKLE INCLUSION PROOFS FOR AUDIT ENTRIES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  §52.B computed a Merkle root but computeMerkleProof was NEVER called for audit.
  Verification re-read every row in a range and rebuilt the whole root — O(n), the
  exact thing Merkle exists to avoid — and an external auditor could not be handed
  a compact proof.

    blockchain.service.js
      auditLeaves(hashes, seqs)               shared, names the offending seq on error
      computeAuditRoot(hashes, {seqs})
      computeAuditProof(hashes, index, {seqs}) -> { leaf, leafHash, proof, steps,
                                                    root, verified }
        `steps` is the fold leaf->root so the UI can render it without a keccak lib.
        `verified` is a server-side self-check via verifyMerkleProof.

    audit-anchor.service.js
      buildProofFromEntries(entries, anchor, seq, expectedEntryHash)   PURE
      getAuditEntryMerkleProof(orgId, seq, expectedEntryHash)          DB + chain

  THREE DESIGN POINTS THAT MATTER:

  1. leafIndex is NOT seq - fromSeq. Sequence gaps exist exactly in the tampering
     case, so subtraction would silently return a proof for the WRONG leaf that
     still looks valid. The range is validated contiguous first, then the index is
     used and re-checked against the row actually at that position.

  2. A proof is REFUSED, not approximated, when the anchored tree cannot be rebuilt:
       - entryCount != toSeq - fromSeq + 1   (legacy sparse anchor)
       - rows.length != entryCount            (rows deleted)
       - rows[i].sequenceNumber != fromSeq+i  (range no longer contiguous)
       - the requested row changed between the two reads
     Without the sparse-anchor guard a forgery was possible: on a legacy anchor
     {fromSeq:1, toSeq:5, entryCount:4} over seqs {1,2,4,5}, deleting seq 4 and
     inserting a new seq 3 carrying the old seq-4 hash rebuilds a BIT-IDENTICAL
     root, and seq 3 came back usable/rootMatch for a row that was never anchored.
     Root cause: seq is not committed into the leaf, so the root proves a sequence
     of hashes, not a seq->hash mapping.

  3. rootMatch alone is NOT proof. It compares against audit_chain_anchors.root_hash,
     an ordinary writable column — inside the same database an attacker with write
     access already controls. getAuditEntryMerkleProof now also calls verifyAuditRoot()
     and returns onChain. The drawer has three states:
        onChain === true   green   proven against Polygon
        onChain === false  red     matches the DB but the root is NOT on-chain
        onChain === null   amber   blockchain disabled, cannot be checked yet

66.E  BREAK FORENSICS — WHICH ROWS, AND WHEN THAT QUESTION HAS NO ANSWER
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  NOTE ON O(log n): descending the tree to localise a break requires the ANCHORED
  intermediate node values. Only the root is stored (and only the root is on-chain),
  so descent is not possible. Storing the layers would not help either — they would
  live in the same database the attacker already controls. Localisation is therefore
  done by the hash chain, which is exact and already available.

    audit.service.js
      collectChainBreaks(entries, head) -> every break, not just the first
        kinds: altered   row content no longer hashes to its stored entryHash
               missing   seq deleted before this row
               reordered seq out of order
               linkage   prevHash does not match the previous surviving row
               head      tail/head disagreement (seq = null)
        A gap suppresses the linkage report it would obviously cause; the `altered`
        self-check still runs because it is independent of neighbours.

    audit-anchor.service.js
      diagnoseAnchor(anchor, rows, chainBreaks)   PURE
      buildBreakReport(orgId)                     one repeatable-read snapshot over
                                                  rows + head + anchors
    GET /api/superadmin/audit/break-report?orgId=

  ATTRIBUTION — each break carries a `culprit`, the seq that was actually modified,
  which is NOT always the seq the break lands on:
      altered@X   -> culprit X    (row X fails its own hash)
      linkage@X   -> culprit X-1  (row X-1 was rewritten WITH a re-hash, so it looks
                                   self-consistent; only its successor's prevHash
                                   still points at the old value)
      head        -> culprit = the tail seq when the head HASH disagrees but the seq
                     matches (the last row was rewritten); null for truncation
      missing     -> culprit null (the deleted seqs are derived from the range)
  Without this, a row rewritten at an anchor's LAST seq threw its only evidence into
  the NEXT anchor: the damaged batch was reported unlocalisable while the intact one
  was accused of holding an altered row.

  THE CLASSIFICATION THAT MATTERS — noRowToBlame. True only when the anchored root
  differs, no hash was unreadable, and nothing is localisable. The UI then says the
  data can no longer identify which row — it may be one row or the whole batch; all
  that survives is proof that a change happened after anchoring. Detectable only
  because of the on-chain record. Deliberately NOT phrased as "the whole batch was
  rewritten": a suffix rewrite produces the same evidence, and overstating it would
  be a claim the data does not support.

  SPARSE LEGACY ANCHORS short-circuit. An anchor whose entryCount does not span its
  seq range predates the gap check in §66.F; its slot map is unknowable, so walking
  fromSeq..toSeq would invent deleted rows that never existed. Such anchors report
  sparseAnchor with empty rowsMissing/rowsAltered and are never classified.

  COMPLEXITY: culprits are bucketed into one Set before the anchor loop. Filtering
  the break list per anchor was O(anchors x breaks) — quadratic, and the degenerate
  case is exactly "everything is broken", which is when the endpoint gets used.

  UI: the Merkle tree in the Audit tab was decorative (it took only leafCount + root
  and drew a generic binary tree). It now takes real leaf hashes, highlights the
  proof path, marks broken leaves red, and its leaves open the proof drawer. A
  forensics panel lists every broken seq with its reason and is click-through.

66.F  CONCURRENCY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Anchoring can fire from three places at once (check tick, flush tick, the
    "Anchor Now" button). It is now serialised by a Postgres advisory lock.
    The lock takes its OWN pg.Client — an earlier per-org version borrowed from the
    shared pool (max 10, no connectionTimeoutMillis) and held it across tx.wait(),
    while drizzle queries inside needed further checkouts from the same pool. Ten
    concurrent anchors for distinct orgs would have wedged the entire application's
    pool permanently. The lock is global rather than per-org, which also removes the
    shared-wallet nonce collision between two concurrent anchorBatch calls.
  - tx.wait(confirmations) had no timeout, so a stuck Polygon transaction held the
    lock forever. It now passes AUDIT_ANCHOR_WAIT_MS (default 600000); ethers
    rejects with TIMEOUT and the finally block releases the lock.
  - The 30-minute check tick and the 6-hour flush tick collided every 6 hours (6h is
    an exact multiple of 30m). Whichever won the lock ran; the other was silently
    dropped, so the flush was skipped for another 6 hours. They are now ONE tick with
    a lastFlush timestamp, updated only after the run resolves, plus a re-entrancy
    guard.
  - audit-recovery restoreMissing() takes the same lock, so a half-restored range
    cannot be anchored. anchorOrgPending() additionally REFUSES a range with gaps —
    anchoring a sparse range bakes a root that can never be reconciled.

66.G  INPUT GUARDS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  hashToBytes32 used ethers.zeroPadValue, which happily LEFT-PADS a truncated hash
  into a valid-looking bytes32 — turning corrupt input into a silently wrong leaf.
  It now rejects anything that is not 64 hex chars. computeAuditRoot wraps the
  failure with the offending seq, and both callers degrade to a readable reason
  instead of a 500: anchorOrgPending returns { anchored:false, benign:false, reason },
  verifyAnchorsForOrg flags that one anchor and keeps checking the rest.

  Anchor results now carry `benign` so the UI can tell "nothing to do" from "this
  failed": a data-integrity failure used to render as a neutral info toast.
  A broken anchor row used to be labelled "not on-chain", which was factually wrong
  (the root IS on chain); it now reads "audit rows corrupt" and shows foundCount.

66.H  TESTS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Six new files under backend/tests/unit/ — audit-chain-head, audit-chain-isolation,
  audit-canonical-roundtrip, audit-root-guard, audit-merkle-proof, audit-break-report.
  Audit + Merkle coverage went from 38 to 136 cases; suite 409/411 (the 2 failures are
  pre-existing: tests/unit/ai-project-tenant and ai-project-text-source cannot resolve
  the npm package `youtube-transcript`, which is not installed).

  ORG ISOLATION is now pinned by tests rather than assumed: two orgs are interleaved
  in one table and org2 is deleted, truncated, tampered and transplanted; org1 must
  stay intact every time. organizationId is inside the hashed payload, so the same
  event under a different org hashes differently and a row cannot be moved between
  tenants without breaking both chains.

  Mutation testing was used to check the tests actually bite. Seven mutants initially
  survived (tail-side coercion removed, case-insensitive hash compare, ok ignoring
  seq, headMatch dropped from early returns, head-missing made a free pass); after
  strengthening, all are killed.

  CAVEAT: backend/.gitignore line 46 ignores tests/, so these six files are NOT
  committed — they live on disk only. The 38 older test files predate the rule and
  remain tracked. Use `git add -f` if they should ship.

66.I  KNOWN GAPS (deliberately not addressed)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - verifyChainForOrg and buildBreakReport read every audit row for the org with no
    LIMIT. Reachable by any org owner via GET /api/activity/integrity. Fine for demo
    data, will not scale.
  - anchorDocument (upload pipeline) does not take the anchoring lock, so it can
    still collide with audit anchoring on the shared wallet nonce. Needs a
    NonceManager.
  - Rows poisoned by the §66.A bugs before this fix cannot be repaired.
  - audit_chain_anchors has no unique constraint on (organization_id, from_seq); the
    advisory lock is the only thing preventing a duplicate anchor.
  - An anchor that succeeds on-chain but fails to record locally is logged with its
    tx hash and returned as a failure, but there is no automatic reconciliation.

66.J  AI DOCUMENTATION DRIFT CORRECTED (same pass, no code change)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Audited §7 (AI Document Analysis) against the code and against §62 (AI Projects
  current state). Four mismatches, all documentation-only:

    - The cover header advertised one model. Four subsystems use four different
      ones, each env-overridable:
        document analysis   AI_ANALYSIS_MODEL        default gpt-5
        project chat        AI_PROJECT_CHAT_MODEL    default gpt-4.1-mini
        studio outputs      AI_PROJECT_OUTPUT_MODEL  default gpt-4.1
        image generation    AI_IMAGE_MODEL           default gpt-image-2
        embeddings          text-embedding-3-small
    - §7 named gpt-4.1-mini for document analysis. The default is gpt-5.
    - §7's grant flow omitted PII redaction entirely. The user picks redactionMode
      'censored' (default) or 'full' AT GRANT TIME; 'censored' runs redactForOutbound
      before embedding, and the choice plus the per-type PII counts are persisted and
      audit-logged. The analyze path redacts again unconditionally. This is §54's
      feature and it was invisible in the flow that describes it.
    - §7 called the upload pipeline 14 steps; it is 15 (§5, STEP 15 in
      upload-pipeline.service.js).
    - §62.4 named gpt-image-1; the constant defaults to gpt-image-2. The code's own
      file-header comment still says gpt-image-1 — left alone, flagged here.

  NOT A DEFECT: ai-project.service.js fails to import in this working tree because
  `youtube-transcript` is absent from node_modules. It IS declared in package.json
  (^1.3.1), so this is a local `npm install` gap, not a manifest or code fault. It is
  the sole cause of the two failing tests reported throughout §66.

  Comments in all files touched by this change are capped at four words, per the
  standing request also noted in §64.5 and §65.4.
